Phase 1 Study:
Decitabine administration had no effect on plasma cytidine levels in Study ASTX727-01.
As expected, on days when cedazuridine was administered in Cycle 1 of Study ASTX727-01, a
consistent increase in plasma cytidine levels was observed. This appeared to be modest
(approximately 2-fold) and transient, as evidenced by the trend of recovery toward baseline by
24 hours, and full recovery as seen at predose on Cycle 2 Day −3.
Phase 2 Study:
The results of the primary PD endpoint (LINE-1 demethylation) are presented below (Table 9
and Figure 3). There was no significant difference between the PD effect on DNA methylation
measured by LINE-1 between oral and IV in Cycles 1 or 2.

The Applicant's Position:

86
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NOA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
The demonstration of equivalent decitabine exposures between ASTX727 and IV decitabine
based on total cycle AUC 0-24 supports the PK bridging approach. Decitabine plasma AUC
exposures are largely considered to be the principal PK determinant of both biological and
clinical activity of decitabine and the ASTX727 5-day AUC was equivalent to the IV decitabine 5­
day AUC (Table 6). Decitabine exerts its hypomethylating activity after incorporation into DNA
during the S-Phase of the cell cycle, and AUC exposures would be consistent with driving
decitabine activity as more cells enter into the S-Phase over the exposure window represented
by AUC. This is also supported by similar PD effect of LINE-1 demethylation between oral
ASTX727 and IV decitabine over the treatment cycle in Phase 3 (Table 8).
The PK/PD analysis demonstrated that a maximal drug effect of LINE-1 demethylation was
achieved for ASTX727 and IV decitabine 20 mg/m2 over the range of decitabine exposures in
the Phase 2 parts of Study ASTX727-01 and in Study ASTX727-02 (Figure 2).
Overall, the PK and biomarker response were similar between ASTX727 and IV decitabine
20 mg/m2, supporting the conclusion that ASTX727 has the same effectiveness as IV decitabine
20 mg/m2.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position on the effectiveness of ASTX727 tablet (35 mg of
decitabine and 100 mg of cedazuridine) when administered orally, once daily for the first 5 days
of each 28-day cycle under fasted conditions, i.e., 2 hours before and 2 hours after dosing.
The Phase 3 study demonstrated comparable 5-day cumulative decitabine exposure (primary
PK endpoint) between ASTX727 and IV decitabine as shown in Table 6, and that the 90%
confidence intervals of the geometric mean ratio were within bioequivalence limits of 80% and
125%.
Comparison of daily decitabine exposures (Table A) demonstrated that following the first dose
of ASTX727, the Day 1 exposure compared to IV decitabine was 40% lower. However, following
5 consecutive once daily doses of ASTX727, the Day 5 decitabine exposure was comparable
between ASTX727 and IV decitabine. Also, the decitabine exposures at Day 2 and Day 5 were
comparable, indicating attainment of steady state at Day 2 for ASTX727. Nonetheless, the
lower Day 1 exposure for ASTX727 in not likely to affect the efficacy, as
•
•
•

decitabine requires at least 4 cycles to show response per the Dacogen USPI, with a median
time to CR+PR response of 162 days,
the efficacy of ASTX727 in the Phase 2 study were comparable to those of Dacogen (see
Sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.4), and
maximum LINE-1 demethylation was observed at Day 8 suggesting a cumulative effect of
exposure.

The final confirmation of the dose selection came from the PK equivalence primary endpoint
analysis in the pivotal Phase 3 study presented above in Table 6 (primary analysis), and Table 7
(sensitivity and secondary analyses). In addition, the levels and range of decitabine exposures
after oral ASTX727 recommended dose largely overlapped with the range of exposures after IV
decitabine standard dose (Figure 1).
Exposure-Safety
An exposure-safety analysis was performed on exposure and adverse event (AE) data from the
Phase 2 part of Study ASTX727-01 and from Study ASTX727-02 (Phase 3). There was no
statistically significant difference in the risk of thrombocytopenia or neutropenia with oral
versus IV decitabine.
The tables and figures below show model-based predictions of probability or the incidence of
the most common adverse events of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia over a range of
decitabine 5-day AUC exposures.

The proposed dosing regimen for ASTX727 is oral administration of 1 tablet (FDC of 100 mg
cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) each day for the fi rst 5 days of each 28-day cycl e. This
regimen replicates t he decitabi ne AUC exposu res of IV decitabine 20 mg/m 2 by 1-hr infusion
each day for t he first 5 days of each 28-day cycl e. As such, this ASTX727 dosing regi men is
appropriate to the genera l patient popu lation for which the indication is being sought.

Dose Modification Strategy: Dose modifications (dose delays/interruptions and dose
reductions) due to hematologic toxicity were common with ASTX727.
The dose delay strategy proposed by the Applicant for hematologic adverse reactions allows for
(b)
dose delays if cytopenias persist after the first <4Weeks of treatment. Patients may continue
dosing if counts recover within :::weeks after dose delay. The Applicant's proposed dose delay
strategy for hemat<~logica l toxicity is reasonable. However, dose delays can be extended to 4
weeks, instead of <4>Neeks, considering that majority of the dose delays were within 28 days
with ASTX727 and o be consistent with dose delay criterion for IV decitabine.
In Study ASTX727-02, 68% (91of133) patients had dose delays or interruptions. The adverse
reactions leading to dose interruptions were mainly cytopenias, particularly neutropenia,
followed by infections and infestations. Majority of the dose-delays occurred early in therapy,
mostly in Cycles 2 to 4, with a short duration (see Table C). Dose delays greater than 14 days
were observed in only 15% of the dose delayed cycles, however, majority were less than 28
days. The dose delays in majority of patients occurred in 1 to 5 cycles (median 2 cycles, range:
0, 15). The complete response {CR) rate in the patient subpopulation with dose delays were
higher than in the overall population in Study ASTX727-02.

The Applica nt' s proposed dose modification strategy for hematological t oxicity incl udes dose
reductions if cytopenias persist for greater t han 2 weeks. The Applica nt proposes a dose
reduction st rat egy that includes reduction to a 4 day regimen/cycle, and if t oxicities persist,
further red uct ion to a 3 day regimen/cycle and fi nally, t o a 3 day, every-other-day regimen per
cycle. The proposed dose reduct ion strategy is reasonable.
In Study ASTX727-02, 50% {66of133) of t he patients had dose reductions (DR), mainly due t o
adverse reactions, count recovery, and myelosuppression. Like dose delays, the adverse
reactions leadi ng to DR were mainly cytopenias, specifically neutropenia. DR occurred as early
as t he 3rd cycle (median time to first DR was 116 days, range: 59, 467) and a med ian duration of
138 days (ra nge: 0, 467 days). The breakdown of the DR in Study ASTX727-02 is list ed in Table D
and Figure D. Majority of patients had first dose reduction to a 4-day regimen per cycle, with
half of t hem continui ng with t he 4-day regimen per cycle. Th irty six percent of patients o n t he
4-day regimen/cycle had a second DR to 3-day regimen/cycle, and 14% of patients had second
DR t o an every alt ernating day 3-day regimen. The CR rat e in t he pat ient subpopulation with
DR was sim ilar to that in t he overall popu lation (23% versus 21%) in Study ASTX727-02.
Considering t he 3-day regimen has higher exposure than the every alternating day 3-day
regimen, it wou ld be beneficial t o dose reduce first from a 4-day t o 3-day regimen, and
subseq uently to an alternating 3-day regimen, if necessary. Although the decitabine exposure
with an alternating day 3-day regimen is on ly about 40% of that with a 5-day regimen, it may be
beneficial to continue decitabi ne treat ment wit h t he 3-day alternating DR regimen for patients
who experience adverse reactions with the 3-day regimen.

6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required
for subpopu lations based on intrinsic patient factors?
Data :
No adjustment to the dosage of ASTX727 is recommended on the basis of age, sex, or weight.
No adjustment to the dosage of ASTX727 is recommended in patients with mi ld or moderate
rena l impairment. A recommended dose has not been established for patients with severe
rena l impairment or patients on dia lysis.
(bf(4J

A recommended dose has not been established for patients with severe hepatic
impa irment.
No clinica l studies were conducted to specifically assess the im pact of intrinsic factors on PK
since the clinical program of ASTX727 included sufficient patient numbers to assess exposure
effect based on most intrinsic factors.

Specific Populations
Age, sex, weight, renal function, and hepatic function were assessed as potentia l covariates in
the PopPK ana lysis using data from the Phase 2 part of Study ASTX727-01 and from
Study ASTX727-02. These studies did not have sufficient racial diversity to inform potentia l
correlations with regard to race or ethnicity.

Age
Age (32 to 90 years; Figure 6) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine
or cedazuridine after administration of ASTX727. Age was not found to be a significant
covariate in the population PK model.

Sex
Sex did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after
administration of ASTX727 (Figure 7).

Body weight (41 to 158 kg) did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or

body weight on decitabine AUC following oral ASTX727, this was not clinically meaningful.

clinically meaningful and did not result in clinically meaningful effect on decitabine exposure.

decitabine or cedazuridine is unknown.

The Applicant’s Position:
Based on the above data age, sex, or mild to moderate hepatic impairment did not have a
clinically meaningful effect on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine after administration of
ASTX727. Mild or moderate renal impairment had an effect on cedazuridine exposures, but this
was not considered to be clinically meaningful.
The effect of severe hepatic impairment or of severe renal impairment on the PK of decitabine
and cedazuridine is unknown.
As a result, dose adjustment is not necessary for intrinsic patient factors, but caution should be
exercised with severe hepatic or severe renal impairment.
Dose reduction of ASTX727 in response to drug-related myelosuppression, if severe and/or
prolonged, could be achieved by reducing the total 5-day decitabine exposures by reducing the
number of doses per cycle.

Effect of Hepatic Impairment: FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no dose
adjustment for patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > ULN to 1.5 x ULN or AST
> ULN). FDA does not agree that there is sufficient information to address the effect of
moderate (total bilirubin >1.5 to 3 x ULN, any AST) and severe (total bilirubin >3 x ULN, any AST)
on decitabine and cedazuridine PK, as there were only 3 patients with moderate hepatic
impairment and no patients with severe hepatic impairment in Studies ASTX727-01-B and
ASTX727-02 (Figure 10). It should be noted that decitabine is mainly eliminated in the liver by
CDA. A PMR to determine the dose recommendation for ASTX727 in moderate and severe
hepatic impairment will be required.
Effect of Renal Impairment: FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no dose modification
is necessary for patients with mild (creatinine clearance (CLcr) 60 to <90 mL/min) and moderate
(CLcr) 30 to <60 mL/min) impairment. As shown in Figure H, increased cedazuridine levels can
increase decitabine exposure. Cedazuridine exposure is also inversely correlated with
creatinine clearance, with increase in exposure with decreasing renal clearance (Figure H).
However, the point estimates for cumulative decitabine exposure between oral and IV
decitabine formulations were similar for patients with mild and moderate renal impairment
(see Table E below). The mean 5-day decitabine exposures for ASTX727 was less than for IV
decitabine for patients with normal renal function, however, the decrease was <20%.

Data:

6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and
what is the appropriate management strategy?

Effect of Food
The food effect study, ASTX727-04, found that there was little effect on the PK of cedazuridine
when ASTX727 was administered with a high-fat meal (approximately 800-1000 calories, with
approximately 150 calories from protein, approximately 250 calories from carbohydrates, and
approximately 500-600 calories from fat). Cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer exposure
parameters were approximately 10% and 20% higher, respectively, with food.
The same food effect study found that there was an effect on the PK of decitabine when
ASTX727 was administered with a high-fat meal (Table 16). The extent and rate of decitabine
exposures were both reduced. Exposure was reduced by approximately 40% (AUC) to 54%
(C max ) and the t max was delayed from 1.00 hour to 1.75 hours when administration was with
food.

AUC 0-8 =area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to 8 hours; AUC 0-24 =area under the

Source: CSR ASTX727-04 PK Report, Table 6.5-5.

Effect of ASTX727 on Other Drugs
No specific study of the effect of cedazuridine on the PK of other drugs has been conducted.
Cedazuridine is a potent inhibitor of CDA (IC 50 =281 nmol/L), so it would affect the metabolism
and exposure of any coadministered drug that is metabolized via this route.
In vitro, cedazuridine (up to 400 μmol/L) did not induce CYP1A, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, and CYP3A.
The fold induction (relative to vehicle) for the CYPs evaluated ranged from 0.8 to 1.5 over the
cedazuridine concentration range tested. A
In vitro, cedazuridine did not inhibit any of the CYP isoforms tested 1A, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19,
2D6, 2E1, and 3A; in all cases, the IC 50 was >400 μmol/L.
Cedazuridine was not an inhibitor of most of the transporters tested: BCRP, BSEP, P-gp, CNT2,
ENT1, ENT2, MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 at concentrations
up to 300 μmol/L. A weak inhibition of CNT2 and CNT3 by cedazuridine was observed at
300 μmol/L, but this is not considered clinically meaningful (cedazuridine clinical plasma C max is
approximately 2 μmol/L).
Drug interaction studies with decitabine have not been conducted. Decitabine is unlikely to
cause DDI via CYP enzymes.
Effect of Other Drugs on ASTX727
No specific study of the effect of other drugs on the PK of cedazuridine has been conducted.
In vitro, cedazuridine was not metabolized by human liver S9 fractions or liver cytosols and was
not a substrate for the majority of transporters. Although cedazuridine was transported by
CNT1, CNT3, and ENT2 (3.81-, 4.58-, 2.63-fold, respectively in MDCKII or HEK293 cells
expressing the transporters), the potential for drug-drug interactions with comedications is low.
Cedazuridine conversion to its major metabolite (cedazuridine-epimer) occurs prior to its
absorption and it was postulated that it may be affected by gastric pH. A PopPK assessment of
concomitant proton pump inhibitor medication as a potential covariate found that such
comedication did not have a significant impact on decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine­
epimer parameters and subsequent exposures, suggesting that drugs modifying gastric pH are
unlikely to affect the PK of cedazuridine or decitabine after ASTX727 administration (Figure 13).

Drug interaction studies with decitabine have not been conducted. Decitabine is not a substrate
for CYP enzymes, and the plasma protein binding of decitabine is negligible.

Effect of Concomitant drug use: FDA agrees with the Applicant' s position that no dose
recommendation for ASTX727 in patients with concomitant drugs and to avoid concomitant
drugs that are metabolized by CDA. In vitro studies suggest that cedazuridine is not a substrate
or modulators of CYP enzymes or major transporters.
FDA noted that there was an increase in dissolution of ASTX727 with increase in pH. The
protocol specifies to avoid gastric acid reducing agents within 4 hours of ASTX727
administration, however, per the Applicant, no specific written instructions were provided to
the patients regarding administration of acid reducing agents. Nonetheless, majority of patients
on proton pump inhibitors (PPI) were on chronic PPI use in Study ASTX727-02.
Given that cedazuridine conversion to its epimer is believed to occur prior to absorption in the
stomach and roughly 30% of patients in the Phase 2 and 3 studies were concomitantly
administered PPI, an independent analysis of patients in Study ASTX727-02 who were and were
not concomitantly administered PPI that overlapped with administration of ASTX727 was
conducted. The analysis (Table I) revealed that the cumulative decitabine exposures were not
different between oral and IV decitabine formulations in patients coadministered with PPI and
patients not taking PPI: the point estimates were close to 1.

6.3.2.5 Does this drug(s) prolong the QT or QTc interval?
Data:
An exposure-response analysis of decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer
concentrations with the change from baseline in heart rate corrected QT interval (QTc) from
Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 was conducted.
The ECGs were conducted at screening and predose on Day 3 (Cycles 1 and 2) and 3 hours
postdose for ASTX727 treatment (time-matched with PK at t max of cedazuridine)and within
10 minutes of the end of infusion for IV decitabine treatment (time-matched with PK at t max of
decitabine) on Day 3.
ECG assessments used for determination of QTc interval were time-matched with the PK blood
draws.
For this analysis, a modified analysis was required because, for each subject, only 2 postdose
ECG-plasma concentration were obtained (1 in each period). Therefore, due to the limited
number of ECG and plasma concentration time point matches, a formal complete
exposure-response analysis was not possible. For this analysis, a general linear modeling
approach was used. Results of the linear regression model are presented in Figure 14
(cedazuridine concentration), Figure 15 (cedazuridine-epimer concentration), Figure 16
(decitabine concentration after oral ASTX727), and Figure 17 (decitabine concentration after IV
decitabine).

The Applicant’s Position:
Astex believes the studies included in the submission provide adequate data to support the
clinical pharmacology, AUC equivalence, efficacy, and safety of ASTX727 in patients with MDS
or CMML. Since the application is primarily based on a PK bridging approach the long-term
effectiveness and safety of ASTX727 are provided from single-arm treatment with ASTX727
after the first 2 randomized cycles. In addition to the systemic decitabine AUC exposure
equivalence and similarity of the biological DNA demethylation effect between ASTX727 and IV
decitabine in the first 2 randomized cycles, we have also used prior decitabine approval
(Dacogen USPI 2019) and the published single-arm study of IV decitabine (5-day regimen) from
Steensma et al 2009 as a reference of the expected effectiveness and safety of IV decitabine
compared to data from ASTX727 treatment in the clinical program.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the summary of Studies presented in Table 17 above. It is notable that Study
ASTX727-02 enrolled patients with all FAB classes as well, which included patients with low-risk
IPSS score.
On Study ASTX727-01-A, the Phase 1, single arm, dose escalation trial, prior azacitidine was
allowed with no limitation on the number of prior cycles. On the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies
(ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02) patients could have only received 1 prior cycle of azacitidine or
decitabine.

NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)

8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
Applicant’s Position:
Extensive nonclinical and clinical information as well as postmarketing information are
available for IV decitabine; therefore, the ASTX727 development program was designed
to generate comprehensive pharmacokinetic (PK) data to bridge ASTX727 to safety and
efficacy evidence for decitabine. This PK bridging approach is in accordance with FDA
guidance for industry, “Providing Clinical Evidence of Effectiveness for Human Drugs and
Biological Products,” dated May 1998. The design of the clinical protocol for Phase 3
study ASTX727-02 was developed based on the end of Phase 2 agreement between
Astex and the FDA.
In addition to the PK bridging to efficacy and safety, the randomized crossover design of
the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies in the first 2 treatment cycles allowed intra-patient
comparison of ASTX727 (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) and IV decitabine
(20 mg/m2), both given once daily for 5 consecutive days (Daily×5), to also assess the PD
effect on global DNA methylation (LINE-1 DNA) and safety, over the first 2 cycles. As
designed, these studies included continuation of treatment with ASTX727 from Cycle 3
onwards, as long as the subject continued to benefit, in order to generate single-arm,
long-term safety and efficacy data for ASTX727 in the treated disease population. The
Phase 1 part of study ASTX727-01 (ASTX727-01-A CSR) was also conducted in MDS and
CMML patients and had the longest follow up (minimum 18 months). All subjects were
treated with ASTX727 in all cycles (except Cycle 1 Day 1 when IV decitabine at 20 mg/m2
was administered) but with different oral doses of cedazuridine (40, 60, and 100 mg)
and decitabine (20, 30, and 40 mg). The main other difference between Phase 1 and the
Phase 2 and 3 studies is that prior azacitidine was allowed in Phase 1 without limitation
of number of prior azacitidine cycles. In Phase 2 and 3, prior decitabine or azacitidine
treatment were limited to only one prior cycle.
Eligible subjects in all 3 studies were males or females ≥18 years of age with de novo or
previously treated (any number of cycles of prior azacitidine in Phase 1 but only up to
1 cycle of azacitidine or decitabine in Phase 2 and 3) Int-1, Int-2, or high-risk MDS or
CMML. Subjects with Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status
(PS) 0-2 were allowed to participate in Phase 1 and 2 but ECOG PS 2 subjects were
excluded from Phase 3 to minimize of number of subjects who may not have been able
to receive at least the first 2 cycles of treatment, necessary for analysis of the primary
endpoint (AUC exposure). In addition, subjects with French-American-British (FAB)
classification of refractory anemia with excess blasts in transformation (bone marrow
130
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
blasts 20% to 30%) were allowed in Phase 3, as these are included in the FDA approved
Dacogen indication, but not in Phase 1 or 2. The duration of treatment and follow-up in
the Phase 1 and 2 studies were also longer (median ~24 months) than in the Phase 3
study (median~5 months). We also note that since the initial studies presented in the
Dacogen USPI only used the FAB classification and IPSS score, we did not collect data on
the MDS and CMML WHO classification in the studies.
Phase 1 and 2 Studies (ASTX727-01-A and B CSRs)
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
Study ASTX727-01 was a Phase 1-2 trial to evaluate ASTX727, for treatment of patients
with MDS/CMML. The Phase 1 part of the trial (ASTX727-01-A CSR) was a single-arm,
dose-finding, dose-escalation design. The Phase 2 part of the trial (ASTX727-01-B CSR)
provided confirmation of the doses selected based on Phase 1 finding.
Phase 1:
The Phase 1 portion of Study ASTX727-01 (CSR ASTX727-01-A) was a first-in-human,
open-label, non-randomized, single-arm, PK-guided, dose-escalation study to assess the
safety, tolerability, and PK profile of oral doses of cedazuridine and decitabine
administered concomitantly as separate capsules. The purpose of the study was to
identify the doses of oral cedazuridine and oral decitabine that achieved decitabine AUC
comparable to that of IV decitabine at the approved dose of 20 mg/m2 Daily×5. In this
study separate cedazuridine capsules (20-mg capsules) and decitabine capsules (5- and
20-mg capsules) were used. The starting dose was 40 mg cedazuridine and 20 mg
decitabine. Doses were escalated using a standard 3+3 design after review of PK and
safety data (Figure 18).
The study design included assessments of the PK of oral decitabine (Cycle 1 Day -3
[C1D-3]), IV decitabine (C1D1), oral cedazuridine and decitabine administered
concomitantly (C1D2 through C1D5), and oral cedazuridine alone (C2D-3). Oral
cedazuridine and decitabine were then administered in 5-day treatment cycles (Days 1
through 5) from Cycle 2 onwards at the same doses used in Cycle 1 (intrapatient dose
escalation was allowed in subsequent cycles). Cedazuridine and decitabine were
administered under fasted conditions (i.e., no food, alcohol, or non-clear liquids for 2
hours before and 2 hours after dosing).

131
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Clinical response was a secondary endpoint in this phase of the study. Efficacy from this
study is supportive since it used different doses of cedazuridine and decitabine but also
because almost half the subjects (46%) received prior azacitidine or decitabine with no
limits in number of prior azacitidine cycles.

When the target level of decitabine AUC after oral administration was within approximately 0.90-1.10 of IV
decitabine AUC with no more than 1 DLT per 6 subjects, the cohort was expanded to reach up to 12-18 total
subjects in the cohort to confirm the AUC range before opening the Dose Confirmation Stage. If the AUC was out of
the desired range, or more than 1 DLT occurred per 6 subjects, the Data and Safety Review Committee could
recommend further adjustment of the cedazuridine or decitabine dose in a new cohort of 3 subjects until the
desired AUC range and safety were achieved in at least 3 subjects.

Phase 2:
Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 (CSR ASTX727-01-B) was a Phase 2, open-label,
randomized, 2-cycle, 2-sequence crossover study followed by a single-arm continuation
of treatment conducted in 2 stages to assess the PK, PD, safety, and efficacy of
cedazuridine (100 mg) and decitabine (35 mg) as separate capsules (in the Dose
Confirmation Stage) and as the ASTX727 FDC tablet (in the FDC Stage) administered
Daily×5. At least 42 subjects were planned to be randomized in the Dose Confirmation
Stage, and 18 to 24 subjects were planned to be randomized in the FDC Stage.
Treatment continued until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, the subject
withdrew consent, or the subject was withdrawn from the study. The study schema is
shown in Figure 19.
A standard 2×2 crossover design during the first 2 cycles of treatment was used for
within-subject comparison of decitabine AUCs and LINE-1 demethylation after
treatment with ASTX727 and IV decitabine. Masking the treatment assignment was not
feasible due to the different routes of administration (oral vs IV).

Trial Location
Study ASTX727-01 was conducted at 15 centers in North America (11 centers in the
United States and 4 centers in Canada).
Choice of Control Group (Phase 2)
The control group in the randomized crossover design was IV decitabine. IV decitabine
(20 mg/m 2 once daily for 5 days) is an approved therapy for MOS/CM ML (Dacogen USPI
2019) and was therefore an appropriate active comparator.
Diagnostic Criteria (Phase 2)
The Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 enrolled adult patients with MOS with

134
Version date: June 11, 2019 (A ll NOA/ BLA reviews)

Reference ID 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
peripheral blasts or bone marrow blasts <20% or CMML who had received no more than
1 prior cycle of an HMA. Diagnosis was based on local centers pathology assessment of
peripheral blood and bone marrow including assessment of the IPSS category (int-1, int­
2, or high-risk) to match the FDA-approved indication for IV decitabine (Dacogen USPI
2019). Data on WHO classification were not collected as part of the study.
Key inclusion criteria:
•

Men or women ≥18 years with IPSS intermediate-1, intermediate-2, or high-risk MDS
with peripheral blasts or bone marrow blasts <20% in all study stages, including
subjects with CMML who are candidates for treatment with an HMA.

•

Subjects with Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status of 0
to 2.

•

Subjects with adequate organ function defined as:
•

Hepatic: Total or direct bilirubin ≤2 × upper limit of normal (ULN); AST/SGOT and
ALT/SGPT ≤2.5 × ULN.

•

Renal: serum creatinine ≤1.5 × ULN or creatinine clearance >50 mL/min/1.73 m2
for subjects with creatinine levels above institutional normal.

•

For subjects with prior allogeneic stem cell transplant, no evidence of graft-versus­
host disease and must be ≥2 weeks off systemic immunosuppressive therapy.

•

Able to swallow the number of capsules required for the treatment assignment within
a 10-minute period and tolerate 4 hours of fasting.

Key exclusion criteria:
•

Previous treatment with at least 2 courses of decitabine or azacitidine.
•

Known or suspected hypersensitivity to decitabine or the components of
cedazuridine.

•

Diagnosis of AML with bone marrow or peripheral blast count ≥20% or other
hematological malignancies.

•

Active uncontrolled gastric or duodenal ulcer.

The diagnosis of MDS and CMML was appropriate matching the IV decitabine FDA
approval. The key inclusion and exclusion criteria were appropriate to ensure subjects
safety (e.g., organ functions) and allow assessment of efficacy of ASTX727 (exclusion of
more than one cycle of prior HMA treatment).

135
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Dose Selection (Phase 2)
Dosing in the Phase 2 portion of Study ASTX727-01 was based on the decitabine AUC
findings from the Phase 1 Dose Escalation portion of Study ASTX727-01. In the Phase 1
Dose Escalation (results reported in CSR ASTX727-01-A), decitabine exposure increased
when decitabine was administered with cedazuridine, compared with decitabine
administered alone, and approached or exceeded the decitabine AUC exposure of IV
decitabine (128% oral/IV) at the highest oral decitabine dose of 40 mg. Oral/IV was 81%
when the decitabine dose was de-escalated to 30 mg. It was therefore concluded from
Phase 1 that an intermediate oral decitabine dose between 30 mg and 40 mg would
best replicate the IV exposure and this was confirmed by PK modelling. The assessment
led to the selection of the doses of the 2 components of ASTX727 to carry into the Phase
2 part of the trial: 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine.
Study Treatments (Phase 2)
Dose Confirmation Stage:
Test Product (cedazuridine and decitabine capsules)
•

Dose: 100 mg cedazuridine (five 20-mg capsules) administered concomitantly with
35 mg decitabine (one 20-mg capsule and three 5-mg capsules) orally once daily on
Days 1 through 5 (Daily×5) in 28-day cycles.

Reference Product (IV decitabine, 5-day regimen)
•

Dose: 20 mg/m2 decitabine by continuous 1-hour IV infusion Daily×5 in 28-day cycles.

FDC Stage:
Test Product (ASTX727 FDC tablet)
•

Dose: one ASTX727 FDC tablet (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine)
administered orally Daily×5 in 28-day cycles.

Reference Product (IV decitabine, 5-day regimen)
•

Dose: 20 mg/m2 decitabine by continuous 1-hour IV infusion Daily×5 in 28-day cycles.

Study drugs (test and reference products) were administered at the study center under
the supervision of study personnel during Cycle 1 and Cycle 2 of the Dose Confirmation
and FDC Stages. In Cycles ≥3 of both stages, the test products (capsules or tablets) were
administered on an outpatient basis. The study drugs (test and reference) were to be
taken in the morning at the same time of day (±1 hour) on each dosing day, in at least the
first 2 cycles of treatment.

136
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Assignment to Treatment (Phase 2)
Individual subject treatment assignments in each study stage were determined through
a computer generated randomization scheme and accessed through the electronic data
capture system used for study data management. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1
ratio and stratified by IPSS risk level (IPSS Int-1 vs IPSS Int-2 and high-risk [subjects with
CMML were randomized into the IPSS Intermediate-2/high risk stratum]) to one of the
following treatment sequences:
•

Sequence A: cedazuridine + oral decitabine capsules (Dose Confirmation Stage) or the
ASTX727 FDC tablet (FDC Stage) in Cycle 1 and then IV decitabine Cycle 2, or

•

Sequence B: IV decitabine Cycle 1 and then cedazuridine + oral decitabine capsules
(Dose Confirmation Stage) or the ASTX727 FDC tablet (FDC Stage) in Cycle 2.
All subjects received ASTX727 from Cycle 3 until treatment discontinuation. The
randomization code and treatment assignment for each subject are provided in the
submission (ASTX727-01-B CSR, Appendix 16.1.7).

Blinding (Phase 2)
This was an open-label study.
The primary PK and PD endpoints (total cycle AUC, LINE-1 methylation), were not
susceptible to bias due to the objective nature of the measurements of PK and the LINE­
1 assay. Efficacy response rate assessment is subject to bias; however, response
assessments were done in Phase 2 by Astex medical personnel following the
International Working Group (IWG) 2006 criteria by medical review of objective
hematology data provided by the center (peripheral blood, bone marrow, and
transfusion data). Efficacy survival data are not subject to bias.
Dose Modification/Dose Discontinuation (Phase 2)
Dose modification was not allowed during the first 2 cycles in order not to bias the PK
and PD assessments. Subjects should receive the full intended dose of cedazuridine
(100 mg) and decitabine (35 mg) as either separate capsules (Dose Confirmation Stage)
or as the ASTX727 FDC tablet (FDC Stage) and IV decitabine dose (20 mg/m2) in the first
2 cycles . Cycle 2 can be delayed until blood count recovery from drug related
hematological toxicity, to allow full dose in Cycle 2.
Guidelines for dose delay or dose reduction in response to toxicity are detailed in the
ASTX727-01 Protocol (Section 7.3). In summary, ASTX727 dose reduction in Phase 2 was
recommended by reducing the number of dosing days from 5 to 4 then 3. There was no
137
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
guidelines for dose modification of IV decitabine as it was administered for only one
cycle.
Administrative Structure (Phase 2)
The Applicant was responsible for all aspects of the study, including oversight of the
contract research organization responsible for study management and monitoring. An
additional organization coordinated monitoring activities and support for Canadian
study centers.
A Data and Safety Review Committee (DSRC) met periodically, mainly during the Phase 1
part of the study, to discuss and agree on the dose escalation for each subsequent
cohort after assessment of safety, PK, and PD data from the previous cohort.
Response was assessed independently by 2 Astex medical monitors as described in
Appendix 1 of the SAP for ASTX727-01-B using local center peripheral blood, bone
marrow, and transfusion data and applying the IWG 2006 response criteria.

INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Dietary Restrictions/Instructions (Phase 2)
Subjects were permitted to have only clear liquids for 2 hours before and 2 hours after
administration of the oral test product (cedazuridine and decitabine capsules or the
ASTX727 FDC tablet). No dietary restrictions were in place when subjects received IV
decitabine.
Concurrent Medications (Phase 2)
Supportive treatment such as antiemetics, blood and platelet transfusions, antibiotics
including antifungal treatment were permitted at the investigator discretion according
to the institution’s standard of practice.
Hematopoietic growth factors were permitted in the event of a life-threatening septic
event, at the discretion of the treating physician. Erythropoietin and/or Neupogen could
be used after Cycle 1 and 2 in Phase 2.
Prohibited concomitant therapies were radiation therapy, chemotherapy,
immunotherapy, or any experimental therapy. Other nucleosides or drugs that are
metabolized by cytidine deaminase were also prohibited when cedazuridine was being
administered.
Medications that may affect gastric pH were allowed, but were recommended not to be
taken within ±4 hours of administration of oral ASTX727.
Treatment Compliance (Phase 2)
During the first 2 cycles, all treatments (oral and IV) were administered by study
personnel at each study center. In Cycles ≥3, test products (capsules or tablets) were
self administered on an outpatient basis. Drug accountability was determined by
counting the number of capsules or tablets dispensed and returned by the subject to
the study center.
Rescue Medications (Phase 2)
No rescue medications per se were planned in the trial apart from the permitted
concomitant medications and supportive treatments described above.
Subject Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal (Phase 2)
Subjects may discontinue study treatment for reasons such as disease progression or
unacceptable toxicity, but they should continue on study follow up to assess AML
transformation and survival. Subjects were considered to have completed the study

Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
when they were withdrawn from the study for any of the following reasons:
•

Death.

•

Withdrawal of consent.

•

Lost to follow up.

Subjects lost to follow up were included in the statistical analysis to the point of last
evaluation. For analyses of survival time and time to progression to AML, subjects were
censored on the last date of contact if lost to follow up prior to reaching a time-to-event
endpoint.
Enrollment continued until the required number of evaluable subjects was reached.
For subjects who discontinued treatment, follow-up procedures and assessments were
conducted at the 30-day follow-up visit and thereafter at approximately 3-month
intervals to assess conversion to AML and to assess overall survival. See Table 18 for
more details.

We agree with the Applicant’s description of the trial design of the Phase 1 and 2
Studies (ASTX727-01-A and -B).
Study Endpoints (Phase 2)
The Applicant’s Description:
The objective of the study was to establish and then confirm the doses of the two
components to be used in the final product (ASTX727 FDC tablet). To that effect Phase 2
described multiple primary endpoints (decitabine AUC for PK; and LINE-1 for treatment
PD effect on DNA methylation both using randomized comparison of IV and oral in the
first 2 cycles of treatment) and response rate from study treatment assessed as a single
arm. The multiple primary endpoints were needed to ensure that the selected oral
ASTX727 doses achieved similar decitabine exposure, similar DNA demethylation
compared to IV decitabine, and that the response rate following multiple cycles of oral
ASTX727 was consistent with expected response rate from IV decitabine. This assurance
was deemed necessary before finalizing the dose for the Phase 3 study, which was
discussed with FDA at the EOP2 meeting.
Primary Endpoints (Phase 2)
•

Mean decitabine AUC (estimated from 5 days of dosing) and mean maximum %LINE-1
demethylation after oral decitabine + cedazuridine compared with IV decitabine
20 mg/m2.
142
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
•

Response rate in all subjects (based on IWG 2006 MDS response criteria).

Secondary Endpoints (Phase 2)
•

Incidence and severity grades of adverse events and clinically significant abnormal
laboratory values.

•

Duration of response, hematological improvement, rate of transfusion independence,
time to AML, and overall survival.

•

Other PK parameters of oral decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer (if
needed).

Exploratory Endpoint (Phase 2)
•

Gene mutations and correlation with response.

The endpoints in Phase 2 were standard endpoints and appropriate based on the main
objective of confirming the final doses of ASTX727 to proceed to Phase 3. While PK, PD,
and response rates were all primary in the Phase 2 part of the ASTX727-01 study, they
have different emphasis for this NDA. The efficacy data in Phase 2 (80 patients
randomized and received any amount of treatment) are considered the primary efficacy
results in this application due to the long follow-up necessary to achieve mature efficacy
assessment, which was achieved in this study (minimum follow-up of 12 months;
median follow up of 24 months, and median number of 7 cycles with a range of 1 to 29
cycles). In contrast, the PK and PD endpoints in Phase 2 are considered supportive in the
application since the sample size in Phase 2 was not designed with sufficient power to
achieve AUC equivalence definition (oral/IV ratio with 90% CI between 80% and 125%).
Phase 1 efficacy data are supportive since it is also a mature study with minimum
follow-up of 18 months (median follow-up ~ 24 months, median cycle 5 (range 1-35).
Response rate in both Phase 1 and 2 was based on the most recent and standard criteria
for MDS response assessment (IWG 2006 criteria). Local center objective laboratory and
clinical data from peripheral blood (PB), bone marrow (BM), and transfusions at baseline
and subsequently were carefully reviewed by Astex medical personnel to assign
response category and did not just rely on investigator judgment.
Efficacy Assessments (Phase 2)
Hematology with peripheral blood counts was assessed at baseline and on Day 1, 8, 15
and 22 during Cycles 1 and 2 and at least on Day 1 in Cycle ≥3. Bone marrow was
assessed at baseline and within 7 days before the start of Cycles 3, 5, and 7; then
according to institution practice as long as the subject was responding.

143
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Evaluation of response was based on IWG 2006 MDS Response Criteria (Cheson et al
2006) and was conducted by 2 Astex medical monitors (independently of each other) by
review of peripheral blood and bone marrow data from each cycle and assisted by
programmed data listings as described in Appendix 1 of the SAP for ASTX727-01-B.
The frequency of assessment (multiple PK samples during treatment days); LINE-1
assessment at baseline and weekly in cycles 1 and 2; the frequency of PB, and bone
marrow assessments at baseline and subsequently were sufficient to capture response
category, duration of response, and time to AML transformation. Subjects were
followed monthly (every 4 weeks) during treatment and every 3 months after treatment
discontinuation to adequately capture survival data. Transfusion requirements were
assessed weekly in the first 2 cycles then on Day 1 of each subsequent cycle.

We agree with the Applicant’s description of the study endpoints of the Phase 2 Study
(ASTX727-01-B).
Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments (Phase 2)
The Applicant’s Description:
The planned analyses for this Phase 2 study are described in two different documents,
the Pharmacometric Analysis Plan (27 August 2018) and the Statistical Analysis Plan (08
August 2018). The Pharmacometric Analysis Plan (PAP) addressed the PK analyses of
plasma decitabine, cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer following single and multiple
once daily oral doses of decitabine and cedazuridine for dose-confirmation and fixeddose combination stages. The planned analyses of AUC exposures of decitabine after
treatment with ASTX727 (administered as individual capsules of cedazuridine and
decitabine in the dose-confirmation stage and as a fixed dose tablet in the FDC stage)
versus IV decitabine, as well as planned analyses of secondary PK parameters, were
described in the PAP. The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) described the planned analyses
of all other clinical and PD endpoints.
Sample Size
In the Dose Confirmation Stage of the study, an intra-subject CV of 0.50 (estimated from
a previous study described in the Dacogen Prescribing Information) was chosen to
calculate the sample size to include in the 2 one-sided equivalence tests of the mean
decitabine AUCs of ASTX727(estimated from 5-days of dosing) compared with IV
decitabine. A sample size of 42 subjects provides 86% power at the statistical
significance level of 0.10, when the true ratio of geometric means is 1.0 and the
equivalence limits for the mean ratio are 0.75 and 1.33. This same sample size of 42 in
the same 2×2 cross-over design achieves 80% power to detect the non-inferiority, with
144
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
respect to the mean maximum %LINE-1 demethylation from baseline, of ASTX727
compared with IV decitabine using a one-sided t-test when the margin of non-inferiority
is -0.05, the true mean difference is 0, the standard deviation (SD) is 0.15 and the
significance level is 0.10. The SD of the paired differences of 0.15 was estimated from a
previous Astex study.
For the FDC Stage, a total of 18-24 evaluable subjects included in the 2 one-sided
equivalence tests for mean oral decitabine AUC compared with mean IV decitabine AUC
provided 75%-88% power at a 10% significance level when the true ratio of means is 1.0,
the coefficient of variation under an unlogged scale is 0.55, and the equivalence limits
for the ratio of means are 0.65 and 1.539.
The 18-24 subjects who completed Cycles 1 and 2 with sufficient LINE-1 data in the
same 2×2 crossover design provided 78%-86% power to detect non-inferiority, with
respect to the mean maximum %LINE-1 demethylation from baseline, of the ASTX727
FDC tablet compared with IV decitabine using a one-sided t-test when the margin of
non-inferiority is -0.075, the true mean difference is 0, the SD is 0.15 and the
significance level is 0.10.
Analysis Populations
Two Pharmacokinetic Analysis Populations were used for analysis of PK data and were
defined as follows:
The Primary PK Analysis Set was used for analyses of the primary study endpoint. This
analysis set included all randomized subjects who receive 2 cycles of treatment and who
had sufficient plasma concentration data to allow AUC 0-t determination for each cycle.
The Complete PK Analysis Set included subjects who received at least one dose of study
drug (cedazuridine + oral decitabine, or ASTX727, or IV decitabine) with sufficient
plasma sample data to assess PK parameters (including unpaired AUC comparison) and
with no protocol deviations affecting assessment of the primary study endpoint.
The following Analysis Sets were used for analysis of all non-PK primary and secondary
endpoints:
The All Subject Analysis Set includes data from all randomized subjects including those
who did not receive any study treatment. This dataset was used to summarize
disposition.
The Efficacy Analysis Set includes data from all randomized subjects who received any
amount of study treatment.

Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
The Safety Analysis Set includes data from all randomized subjects who received any
amount of study treatment. In the safety analysis, no data exclusion was allowed
because of protocol deviations.
The PD LINE-1 Analysis Set includes data from subjects if they received at least one
cycle (Days 1 through 5) of study treatment and had LINE-1 methylation data at baseline
(Day 1) and on either Day 8 or Day 15. Based on prior experience with decitabine
treatment, maximum effect on LINE-1 % methylation is mostly achieved on Day 8 or Day
15 following in the 5-day dosing schedule.
Methods for Handling Missing Data (Phase 2)
Handling of Missing Plasma Concentrations
For the PK parameter calculations, imputation of missing data may be done for:
• In instances where there is a missing plasma concentration at the end time of the
predefined partial AUC (i.e., AUC 0-24 , AUC 0-8 ), the following data extrapolation /
interpolation was applied:
o If the predefined end time falls within the range of the available data but does not
coincide with an observed data point, then the plasma concentration corresponding
to the end time of the predefined partial AUC will be calculated (imputed) by
performing a linear or logarithmic interpolation, as applicable.
o If the sample was collected before the end time of the predefined partial AUC (e.g.,
23.5 h for AUC 0-24 ) and corresponds to the last sample collected for the predefined
partial AUC, then the plasma concentration corresponding to the end time of the
predefined partial AUC will be calculated by extrapolation using λz (i.e.,
concentration at 24 h for AUC 0-24 ).
o If λz is not estimable, the observed partial AUC up to the time of the last sample
preceding the end time of the pre-defined partial AUC will be used only if the actual
end time of the predefined partial AUC is within 20% time window of the nominal
end time (i.e. >6.4h for AUC 0-8 and >19.2h for AUC 0-24 ).
o Otherwise, the predefined partial AUC will not be calculated.
• Missing pre-dose values:
o The pre-dose concentration will be set to 0 for the first dose.
o After multiples doses, the pre-dose concentration will be set to Cmin following the
previous dose for cedazuridine and zero for decitabine.
Unless otherwise noted, data summaries will be performed only on observed data.
Incomplete Dosing Information
In the case where subjects were dosed with any of the study drugs but discontinued from
the study (early termination), the concentration data will be reported in the concentration
tables and included in all summary statistics and PK analysis if sufficient concentration data
are available to permit PK analysis for that treatment in that subject.

146
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Missing Sampling Dates and/or Times
For concentration-time profiles from subjects with missing sample dates and/or times, the
nominal sampling day and/or time (as appropriate) will be assumed for the calculation of PK
parameters.
Unexpected Data
A visual inspection of concentration-time profiles will be performed to determine dataset
integrity and potential outliers. Descriptive statistics of PK parameters may be calculated
with and without subjects who could be potential outliers. Subjects with potential outlier
(b) (4)
data will be reviewed by
and the Sponsor for inclusion or
exclusion from the descriptive statistics on a case-by-case basis.
Non-zero predose concentration values will be identified in the study report and included in
the computation of PK parameters. Concentration profiles and PK parameters for subjects
(b) (4)
and
with non-zero predose concentrations greater than 5% of Cmax will be reviewed by
the Sponsor for inclusion or exclusion from the descriptive statistics on a case-by-case basis.
Any plasma concentrations that cannot be uniquely and unequivocally attributed to a
particular subject, period and/or time point based on case report form (CRF) records or
other observed data records in the study will be treated as incomplete data. Such data will
be compiled, commented, and listed separately in the appendix of the report. Incomplete
and/or non-compliant data, if any, will be excluded from analysis datasets; no analyses,
summaries or graphs will be produced using these data.
Vomiting
For subjects receiving, oral decitabine or combination treatment of decitabine and
cedazuridine who may experience emesis on the day or during the serial sampling, if
vomiting occurs at or before 2 times median tmax of cedazuridine or decitabine (whichever
median tmax occurs later), then subject data for decitabine, cedazuridine and its circulating
metabolites (E7727-epimer) will be excluded from PK statistical analysis for the treatment in
which this occurs.

Patient Disposition (Phase 2)
Data:
A total of 86 subjects were randomized in the Phase 2 portion of the study from
11 centers in the US and 4 in Canada. The Efficacy Analysis Set and the Safety Analysis
Set were composed of the 80 randomized subjects who received any amount of study
treatment, no treated subjects were excluded. Six subjects were randomized but did not
receive any treatment (1 died before receiving treatment, 1 because of pathologist
change of diagnosis from MDS to myelofibrosis, 1 withdrew because of disease
progression, 1 withdrew consent before treatment, and 2 withdrew due to elevated
liver function tests). It is to be noted that appendix 16.2.3 of the submitted Phase 1 and
2 CSRs (ASTX727-01-A and ASTX727-01-B) was mislabeled “subjects excluded from
efficacy analysis” when in fact they are listing of subjects who failed screening and were
not enrolled/randomized to the study.
As of the Phase 2 CSR data cutoff date (05 June 2018), 13 subjects were alive and
ongoing treatment. The median duration of follow up was 729 days (~24 months) with a
minimum of 12 months (range, 365 to 876 days). The median duration of treatment was
7 treatment cycles (range, 1 to 29 cycles).
As the FDC stage began after enrollment in the Dose Confirmation Stage had finished,
the median follow up time in the Dose Confirmation Stage (809.5 days) was longer than
in the FDC Stage (460 days).

The Applicant’s Position:
None of the subjects who received treatment were excluded from planned efficacy or
safety analyses. Since efficacy in Phase 2 was assessed as a single arm, it is appropriate
to conduct the efficacy assessment based on subjects who actually received treatment.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA performed an analysis of disposition on the Phase 2 Study ASTX727-01-B using data
from the 120-day safety update received on 4/6/2020. The 120-day safety update used
a cutoff date of 11/1/2019. For the purposes of determining reasons for treatment
discontinuation, FDA considers death under the primary reason for discontinuation (i.e.,
either primary disease or adverse event). Thus, the reason “adverse event” included 9
deaths due to an adverse event, in addition to the disposition events coded as adverse
event. The reason “primary disease” included 2 deaths due to primary disease, in
addition to the disposition events coded as progressive disease.

Protocol Violations/Deviations (Phase 2)
Data :
Categories of important protocol deviations were identified by the study team during
protocol deviation review throughout the study.
Important Protocol Deviations: A subset of protocol deviations that might significantly
affect the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliabi lity of the study data or that might
significantly affect a subject's rights, safety, or well-bei ng.
For this study, important protocol deviations were further defined as those that
potentially affected eva luation of the study endpoints, particu larly the PK primary
endpoint in each study stage . Deviations such as out-of-window assessment/procedures
were not considered important; in addition, PK sampling out-of-window was not
considered significant, as the actual (not the scheduled) PK sampling time was used to
compensate and was considered sufficient for assessment of the primary study
endpoint.
Six deviations were considered to be important and are described by deviation category
as follows:

Safety: 2 occurrences of study center failure to report an SAE to the sponsor with 24

the study in favor of the study drug. Therefore, all patients, including those with
important protocol deviations, were included in our analysis of efficacy endpoints.

The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the demographic characteristics of subjects in this study is representative of
MDS and CMML patients in the general population. Most subjects were elderly (median
age >70 years), males, and predominantly white. The study included a broad range of
BSA (1.3 to 2.4 m2).
No differences in demographic characteristics between subjects in Sequence A and
Sequence B were apparent in Phase 2. Efficacy endpoints were not compared between
treatment sequences. Also, demographic characteristics are not shown here by Phase 2
study stage (Dose Confirmation Stage using separate capsule and FDC Stage using FDC
tablets) since efficacy endpoints are not shown here between the 2 stages. Since all
subjects received the same doses of ASTX727 and all of them received no more than 1
cycle of IV decitabine there was no clinical reason to present efficacy by sequence or by

The Applicant’s Position:
The baseline disease characteristics of subjects in this study are representative of the
patient population intended to receive the marketed product. Most IPSS risk categories
were represented and patients with CMML were also included. No clinically meaningful
differences in baseline characteristics between the study stages or the 2 treatment
sequences were apparent.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
The mean time since diagnosis was 299.3 days and median time since diagnosis was
52.5 days. Of note, this is shorter than the mean and median time since diagnosis noted
in the IV decitabine label (mean 86 weeks and median 29 weeks on the decitabine arm
of the randomized trial and mean 444 days and median 154 days on the single-arm
trial).
Only 7 (9%) received up to 1 cycle of prior HMA therapy prior to enrolling on the trial.

Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use (Phase 2)
Data:
During the 2-way crossover, 73 of the 80 subjects treated (91.3%) received the first 2
cycles of treatment.
The median number of cycles received overall in Phase 2 was 7 (range, 1 to 29) and 50
subjects (62.5%) received 6 or more cycles. Overall in Phase 2, 51.3% of subjects had
delayed cycles and 40% of subjects had dose-reduced cycles (Table 23).

The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment compliance was well controlled/managed at the study centers during the
first 2 cycles as all treatments (oral and IV) were administered by study center
personnel. Compliance with treatment was adequate based on the median number of
7 cycles (range 1, 29), which is consistent with prior IV decitabine 5-day regimen study:
median of 5 cycles (range 1, 17) (Steensma et al 2009).

The Applicant’s Position:
PK: The primary PK objectives of this study were achieved and demonstrated that the
dose of 100 mg cedazuridine combined with 35 mg decitabine produced decitabine
exposures (5-day AUC) that were highly comparable with 20 mg/m2 IV decitabine,
regardless of the formulation administered (Table 12 and Table 13). Therefore, the
efficacy results presented here include the overall Phase 2 population (N=80). The 80%
CI of the oral/IV AUC were within the limits defined in the study analysis plan for each of
the Dose Confirmation stage and the FDC stage.
PD: Oral administration of ASTX727 achieved LINE-1 DNA demethylation similar to IV
decitabine at 20 mg/m2 (Table 9, Figure 3)
Efficacy: The efficacy endpoint of response rate in this study was not compared
between treatment sequences (Sequence A and B). There was no clinical reason to
conduct such a comparison since all patients in the trial only received ASTX727 except
for one cycle of IV decitabine, and all patients received the same doses of cedazuridine
and decitabine in ASTX727.
The CR and overall response rates of 21% and 60%, respectively, overall in Phase 2
(Table 24), were consistent with historical results observed with IV decitabine using the
same IWG 2006 criteria (CR of 17% and overall response of 51% in Steensma et al 2009).
Additional analysis of response by age, sex, country, race, disease type (MDS and
162
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NOA Mu lti-disciplinary Review and Eva luation
NOA 212576, Origina l
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
CMML), and IPSS score showed that ASTX727 was clinically active in all patient
subgroups within the limitation of patient numbers in certain subgroups.
The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
See Section 6 of the Assessment Aid for FDA's assessment of PK and PD resu lts from the
Phase 2 trial.
The acceptable response rate for MOS appropriate for regulatory decision making is
overall response rate (ORR), defined as CR+PR responses. ORR served as the basis for
approval of IV decitabine. There were no PR responses on the Phase 2 trial of ASTX727,
and therefore, on ly CR responses will be considered for demonstration of clinical
benefit. Lesser responses than CR/PR do not convey clear clinical benefit to patients and
will not be included in labeling.
We disagree with the Applicant's assessment of the CR rate. FDA adjudicated CR
responses based on evaluation of bone marrow and laboratory results in the
submission. We could not confirm CR responses in the following subjects:

in the USPI for IV decitabine, which was 17% for the randomized trial and 16% for the
single arm trial (CR rates of 9% and 15%, respectively).
The Applicant confirmed FDA's analysis of the CR results and provided updated efficacy
data including the FDA-adjudicated CR responses with the 120-day safety update.
Of note, the protocol used IWG 2006 MOS Response Criteria, which differs from that
used in the IV decitabine prescribing information (IWG 2000 MOS Response Criteria)
(Cheson, Bennett et al. 2000). The major differences are that the IWG 2000 criteria
required an ANC of 1.5 Gi/L or more with no dysplasia for CR responses, whereas the
2006 criteria on ly required an ANC of 1 Gi/L or more with allowance of persistent
dysplasia. Therefore, the CR responses reported in the decitabine USPI of 9-15% would
likely be higher using the updated response criteria.

Data Quality and Integrity (Phase 2)
Data:
The Astex Quality Assurance group audited 7 study centers, with a total of 8 investigator
audits performed from the 15 centers that randomized subjects (one center was audited
twice). No critical audit findings were identified. Appropriate corrective and preventive
actions were taken for all audit findings.
The data in this submission are in Electronic Common Document (eCTD) format, in
accordance with FDA guidance on electronic submission. The data sets are well
documented and include definition files. The data sets, clinical study reports, summary
of clinical safety, and summary of clinical efficacy are located in Modules 5 and 2 of the
eCTD. \\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDAnnnnn.
The Applicant’s Position:
No data quality or data integrity issues were identified. Astex believes the data and
datasets required for the Agency to be able to conduct their analysis have been
provided in the submission.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
No issues were identified with the data quality or integrity from the Phase 2 study which
could affect the efficacy results, aside from the issues with the response assessments
noted above. The submitted datasets are generally consistent, and variables are clearly
labeled and/or explained.
We agree with the Applicant’s position from a statistical point of view.

Time to AML or Death (Phase 2)
Of the 80 treated subjects, 58.8% reached the event (AML or death), and data were censored for the
remaining 41.3% of subjects. Median time to AML or death was 364 days. Figure 20 shows a plot of the
Kaplan-Meier analysis.

The Applicant’s Position:
The ASTX727 clinical program was designed to support PK bridging of ASTX727 at the
selected doses of 100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine with IV decitabine dose of
20 mg/m2 in a 5-day regimen. However, data from Phase 1 supports clinical activity at a
broad range of doses (cedazuridine 40 to 100 mg) and decitabine (20 to 40 mg) within
the limitation of the small number of patients at each dose level and the large
percentage of patients with prior HMA treatment in the Phase 1 study.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s overall assessment. FDA did not independently
adjudicate responses on the Phase 1 trial given the exploratory nature of the efficacy
findings on this trial.
Durability of Response (Phase 2)
Data:
Duration of response was analyzed based on the subjects who achieved a CR or mCR

using both K-M method and simple descriptive statistics. In the K-M method, subjects’

The median duration of response from the K-M method was 399 days for subjects with a
best response of CR (IQR 195, 413) and was 397 days (IQR 170, 560) for subjects with a
best response of mCR.
Using descriptive statistics, the median (min, max) duration of response was 286 days
(range 32, 553) for subjects with a best response of CR and was 143 days (range 49, 662)

The Applicant submitted an updated ADEFF.xpt file with the 120 day safety update,
which included FDA’s adjudicated dates of response in the four patients noted above.
Using the updated efficacy data, we determined a median duration of CR of 265 days
(range 35 – 553 days). This is equivalent to 8.7 months (range 1.1 – 18.2 months).
Persistence of Effect (Phase 2)
Data:
Median duration of response for Phase 2 subjects with best response of CR was >1 year
(399 days) and was 397 days for subjects with best response of mCR. Twelve subjects
(15%) proceeded to transplant (bone marrow/stem cell transplant). Median overall
survival time (as of the data cutoff date) was 549 days (18.3 months).
The Applicant’s Position:
As with most anticancer therapies, loss of therapeutic effect eventually occurs as
resistance to the drug’s mechanism of action eventually develops; however, responses
to ASTX727 were durable, as noted above.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
As noted above, we determined that median duration of response for phase 2 subjects
with best response of CR was 8.7 months (range 1.1 – 18.2 months).
We confirm that 12 (15%) of patients proceeded to transplant and that median OS was
549 days.
Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints (Phase 2)
Data:
There were no study endpoints evaluating patient-reported outcomes in this study.
The Applicant’s Position:
Not applicable
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s position.
Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial (Phase 2)
Data:
The median (range) time to response for CR was 147 days (29-571) and was 64 days
174
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
(49-218) for mCR; mCR was therefore achieved earlier but reaching a complete response
requires multiple cycles (approximately 4 to 6 cycles consistent with median time to CR
of 147 days).
Exploratory analysis for the presence of mutations in 7 relevant genes (CBL, IDH2,
ASXL1, DNMT3A, TP53, RUNX1, and SF3B1) in the baseline bone marrow of 33 subjects
was performed, and the presence of mutations or suspicious variants were correlated
with response. Of the 14 subjects who had the RUNX1 mutation, 6 (42.9%) had a best
response of CR. None of the mutations for the remaining 6 genes was correlated with
response.
A K-M analysis of OS by gene mutation status for the 3 genes (RUNX1, ASXL1, and TP53)
with the highest number of subjects with identified mutations was performed. No
definitive conclusions can be made due to the small sample size, although median
survival time appeared to be lower for the mutant vs. wild type for the 3 genes shown
(particularly for the TP53 mutation).
The Applicant’s Position:
Achieving a best response of CR usually requires 4 to 6 cycles (median time to CR
147 days). The exploratory analysis of response and survival by gene mutation is
inconclusive due the small number of patients with available mutation data in the study.
It does seem to confirm the known poor survival prognosis of TP53 mutation.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
Using the updated response files submitted with the 120-day safety update (correcting
the dates of CR response per FDA adjudication), the median time to CR was unchanged
at 147 days (range 51 - 303 days). This is equivalent to 4.8 months (range 1.7 - 10.0
months).
We performed an analysis of FDA-adjudicated CR responses in the 33 patients with
available genomic data at baseline:
• RUNX1 (n=14): CR 5 (36%)
• ASXL1 (n=13): CR 2 (15%)
• TP53 (n=5): CR 0 (0%)
• SF3B1 (n=2): CR 0 (0%)
• DNMT3A (n=1): CR 0 (0%)
• IDH2 (n=1): CR 0 (0%)
These analyses are considered exploratory only. The small sample size of the subgroups
explored limits the statistical power of such inferences.

Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 (CSR ASTX727-02) design was identical to Phase 2, and was
an open-label, randomized, 2-cycle, 2-sequence crossover study followed by a singlearm continuation of treatment to assess the PK, PD, safety, and efficacy of the ASTX727
FDC tablet (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) Daily×5 and IV decitabine
20 mg/m2 Daily×5. At least 118 subjects were planned to be randomized. Treatment
continued until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, treatment discontinuation
for other reasons, or withdrawal of consent. The study schema is the same as Phase 2
(Figure 19), all patients received ASTX727 as FDC tablet.
Trial Location (Phase 3)
Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 was conducted at 37 centers in North America (30 centers in
the United States and 7 centers in Canada).
Choice of Control Group (Phase 3)
The purpose of this study was to establish the total 5-day cycle decitabine AUC
equivalence of ASTX727 and IV decitabine in adult patients with MDS or CMML.
Intravenous decitabine (20 mg/m2 once daily for 5 days) is an approved therapy for
MDS/CMML (Dacogen USPI 2019) and was therefore the appropriate active comparator.
Diagnostic Criteria (Phase 3)
Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02 enrolled adult patients with MDS or CMML who had no more
than 1 prior cycle of an HMA. Eligibility criteria were similar to Phase 2 with few
exceptions. Eligible MDS patients were eligible by either FAB or IPSS criteria to match
the Dacogen US label. ECOG PS 2 and other poor general condition criteria (recent
hospitalization for infection, proliferative disease, or life expectancy <12 weeks) were
excluded in Phase 3 in an effort to minimize the number of patients who may not be
able to receive 2 complete cycles of treatment with sufficient PK assessment to be
evaluable for the primary endpoint of AUC equivalence.

176
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Key inclusion criteria:
•

Men or women ≥18 years who are candidates to receive IV decitabine, i.e., subjects
with MDS previously treated or untreated with de novo or secondary MDS, including
all French-American-British subtypes (refractory anemia, refractory anemia with
ringed sideroblasts, refractory anemia with excess blasts, refractory anemia with
excess blasts in transformation, and CMML), and subjects with MDS IPSS int-1, -2, or
high-risk MDS.

•

Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status of 0 to 1.

•

Adequate organ function defined as follows:

•

•

Hepatic: Total or direct bilirubin ≤2 × ULN; AST/SGOT and ALT/SGPT ≤2.5 × ULN.

•

Renal: serum creatinine ≤1.5 × ULN or calculated creatinine clearance or
glomerular filtration rate >50 mL/min/1.73 m2 for subjects with creatinine levels
above institutional normal.

Life expectancy of at least 3 months.

Key exclusion criteria:
•

Prior treatment with more than 1 cycle of azacitidine or decitabine.

•

Hospitalization for more than 2 days for documented febrile neutropenia,
pneumonia, sepsis, or systemic infection in the 30 days before screening.

•

Treatment with any investigational drug or therapy within 2 weeks of study
treatment, or 5 half-lives, whichever is longer, before the first dose of study
treatment, or ongoing clinically significant AEs from previous treatment.

•

Cytotoxic chemotherapy or prior azacitidine or decitabine within 4 weeks of first
dose of study treatment.

•

Concurrent MDS therapies, including lenalidomide, erythropoietin,
cyclosporine/tacrolimus, granulocyte-colony stimulating factor, granulocyte­
macrophage colony-stimulating factor, etc. (Prior treatment with these agents is
permitted, provided that completion is at least 1 week before the first dose of study
treatment.)

•

Rapidly progressive or highly proliferative disease (total white blood cell count of
>15 × 109/L) or other criteria that render the subject at high risk of requiring
intensive cytotoxic chemotherapy within the next 3 months.

Other than the relatively small differences between Phase 2 and 3 eligibility, the
diagnostic criteria and eligibility criteria were appropriate for assessment of the
treatment effect.

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Dose Selection (Phase 3)
Dose selection for Phase 3 was the same dose tested in Phase 2 and discussed above in
Section8.1.1.
Study Treatments (Phase 3)
•

Test Product: ASTX727 FDC tablet (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine).
•

•

Dose: one ASTX727 tablet administered orally once daily on Days 1 through 5
(Daily×5) in 28-day cycles.

Reference Product (IV decitabine 5-day regimen).
•

Dose: 20 mg/m2 continuous 1-hour IV infusion Daily×5 in Cycle 1 or 2.

Study drugs (ASTX727 FDC tablets and IV decitabine) were administered by study center
personnel in Cycles 1 and 2. The actual doses ASTX727 and IV decitabine administered
were recorded in the CRF.
In Cycles ≥3, ASTX727 was administered at the study center on Day 1 of each cycle, and
subjects were given the remaining 4 ASTX727 tablets in a 5-count bottle for home dosing;
subjects were also given IRB approved home dosing logs (diaries) to record home dosing
of ASTX727.
Assignment to Treatment (Phase 3)
Individual subject treatment assignments were determined through a computergenerated randomization schedule and accessed through an interactive web response
system. No stratification was performed. Subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to one
of the following treatment sequences:
•

Sequence A: ASTX727 tablet in Cycle 1 and then IV decitabine in Cycle 2, or

•

Sequence B: IV decitabine in Cycle 1 and then ASTX727 tablet in Cycle 2.

The randomization and treatment assignment for each subject are provided in the
submission (ASTX727-02 CSR, Appendix 16.1.7).
Blinding (Phase 3)
This is an open-label study.
Dose Modification / Dose Discontinuation (Phase 3)
Guidelines for adjusting or withholding study treatment are detailed in the protocol for
178
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
ASTX727-02 (Section 7.6). In summary, dose reductions were not allowed in Cycles 1
and 2. If a dose reduction was necessary in Cycles ≥3, the dose was reduced by
decreasing the number of dosing days in a cycle to 4; if further reduction was necessary,
the dose could have been reduced to 3 days. Dose delays up to 2 weeks were allowed
for recovery of blood counts from drug-related myelosuppression.
Administrative Structure (Phase 3)
The Applicant was responsible for all aspects of the study, including oversight of the
contract research organizations overseeing study management activities in the US and
Canada.
An Independent Review Committee composed of 3 disease experts (who did not
participate as investigators in the study) was established for independent assessment of
clinical response.

The schedule of events for the study is shown below in Table 31.

Dietary Restrictions/Instructions (Phase 3)
The ASTX727 tablet was to be taken at the same time on each dosing day, with water.
Subjects were instructed to fast from food and non-clear liquids for at least 2 hours
before and 2 hours after dosing. No dietary restrictions were in place when subjects
received IV decitabine.
Concurrent Medications (Phase 3)
Antibiotics were permitted to prevent or manage febrile neutropenia. Hematopoietic
growth factors were not to have been routinely used. However, short-term use of G-CSF
for febrile neutropenia was permitted.
Drugs that alter gastric pH were allowed but subjects instructed not to take them
within ±4 hours of ASTX727 administration. Other prohibited concomitant therapies
were chemotherapy, immunotherapy, or any experimental therapy. Other nucleosides
or drugs that are metabolized by CDA were not to have been administered on ASTX727
dosing days.
Treatment Compliance (Phase 3)
Study treatment was administered by study center personnel in Cycles 1 and 2. In
Cycles ≥3, ASTX727 was administered at the study center on Day 1 of each cycle, and
subjects were given the remaining 4 ASTX727 tablets for home dosing; subjects were
also given IRB-approved home dosing diaries to record home dosing of ASTX727. At
their next visit, subjects returned the home dosing log and any unused tablets (if a dose
reduction occurred) to the study center and study staff entered dosing information into
the CRF.
Rescue Medications (Phase 3)
No rescue medications per se were planned in the trial apart from the permitted
concomitant medications and supportive treatments described above.
Subject Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal (Phase 3)
Subjects were considered to have completed the study when they were withdrawn from
the study for any of the following reasons:
•

Death.

•

Withdrawal of consent.

•

Lost to follow up.
