INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Subjects lost to follow up were included in statistical analyses to the point of the data
cut-off date for the CSR.
Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s description of the trial design of the Phase 3 Study
(ASTX727-02).
Study Endpoints (Phase 3)
The Applicant’s Description:
Primary Endpoint (Phase 3)
Comparison between ASTX727 and IV decitabine:
•

Total 5-day AUC exposures of decitabine after treatment with ASTX727 versus IV
decitabine (based on AUC 0-24 ).

Secondary Endpoints
•

Safety as assessed by AEs, concomitant medications, physical examination, clinical
laboratory tests (hematology, serum chemistry, and urinalysis), vital signs, ECOG
performance status, and electrocardiogram.

•

Maximum %LINE-1 demethylation.

•

Additional secondary PK parameters.

•

Clinical response (CR, mCR, PR, and HI) based on International Working Group (IWG)
2006 MDS response criteria.

•

Red blood cell (RBC) or platelet transfusion independence (TI).

•

Leukemia-free survival, defined as the number of days from the date of randomization
to the date when bone marrow or peripheral blood blasts reach ≥20%, or death from
any cause.

•

Overall survival, defined as the number of days from the date of randomization to the
date of death from any cause.

The primary endpoint for the study was based on agreement with FDA at EOP2 meeting
on using a PK bridging approach to primarily establish efficacy. The 5-day total cycle
AUC 0-24 was agreed upon during the pharmacometric analysis plan assessment by FDA.
PD assessments of DNA methylation by LINE-1 assay is appropriate for quantitative
183
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
comparison of treatment effect on global DNA methylation. Safety assessment from both
the randomized first 2 cycles with IV decitabine and long-term from continued ASTX727
treatment is appropriate to assess ASTX727 safety. Finally, efficacy endpoints were
secondary in the trial to assess consistency with efficacy previously reported with IV
decitabine. At the time of the data cutoff for the CSR, the Phase 3 trial did not have
sufficient follow-up (minimum 2 months, median 5 months) to assess response in all
treated patients or to assess long-term efficacy such as duration of response, time to AML,
or overall survival. These endpoints will be later updated after a minimum follow-up of
12 months.
Efficacy Assessments (Phase 3)
Efficacy assessments were almost identical to assessments previously summarized in
Phase 2 (Section 8.1.1 above).
Evaluation of response was based on IWG 2006 MDS Response Criteria (Cheson et al
2006) like in Phase 2; however, response evaluation in Phase 3 was independently
conducted by a panel of 3 external expert medical reviewers who did not participate as
investigators in the study based on database listings of hematology results (peripheral
blood and bone marrow) and transfusion data.
Additional details regarding assessment of clinical response is provided in the
Independent Clinical Response Assessors Charter (ASTX727-02 CSR, Appendix 16.1.13.7).
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s description of the study endpoints of the Phase 3 Study
(ASTX727-02).
Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments (Phase 3)
The Applicant’s Description:
The analyses for this Phase 3 study are described in two different documents, the
Pharmacometric Analysis Plan (06 December 2018) and the Statistical Analysis Plan (27
February 2019). The Pharmacometric Analysis Plan addressed the PK analyses of plasma
decitabine, cedazuridine and cedazuridine-epimer following single and multiple once
daily oral doses of decitabine and cedazuridine. The planned analyses of the primary
endpoint, total 5-day AUC 0-24 exposures of decitabine after treatment with ASTX727
versus IV decitabine, were described in the PAP as well as all planned analyses of the
secondary PK parameters. The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) described the planned
analyses of all other secondary endpoints.

184
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Sample Size
The sample size calculation was based on preliminary analysis of 5-day AUC in study
ASTX727-01, which showed an estimate of intra-subject CV of 0.5. A conservative CV
value of 0.55 was chosen to calculate the sample size to include in the 2 one-sided
equivalence tests for the geometric mean ratio of ASTX727 5-day AUC relative to IV
decitabine 5-day AUC to provide 90% power at the statistical significance level of 0.05,
when the true ratio of geometric means is 1.0, the CV under an unlogged scale is 0.55,
and the 90% CI equivalence limits for the ratio of geometric means are 0.8 and 1.25. It
was assumed that 10% of subjects may not be evaluable in for the primary PK analysis.
Analysis Populations
Two Pharmacokinetic Analysis Populations were used for analysis of PK data and were
defined as follows:
The Primary Endpoint PK Analysis Population was used for calculating decitabine 5-day
(total cycle) AUC 0-24 and equivalence statistical analyses. This analysis set included
decitabine daily AUC 0-24 from subjects who received 5-day dosing of ASTX727 and IV
decitabine in Cycles 1 and 2 and who met the following criteria for both ASTX727 and IV
decitabine dosing and PK assessments:
• ASTX727: received full dose of ASTX727 (i.e., Days 1 through 5) within 3 hours of the
intended dosing time, and no vomiting occurred within 6 hours of dosing.
•

IV decitabine: received the full dose as a 1-hour infusion.

•

Subjects in this analysis set had at least 1 evaluable day of decitabine AUC 0-24
measurement in the IV decitabine cycle, either Day 1 or Day 5. When both days were
available, the average value was used to calculate total 5-day AUC 0-24 . If data from 1
of the 2 available IV days were deemed to be extreme outlier and excluded, the data
from the other IV day (Day 1 or Day 5) was used for analysis.

The Overall PK Analysis Population included additional subjects who may not have
been included in the Primary Endpoint PK Analysis Set and who:
• Received any amount of study treatment (including subjects who received only 1
cycle of treatment).
•

Complied with the protocol sufficiently to ensure PK samples were collected as
intended.

•

Provided sufficient samples to measure available plasma concentrations for
decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer.

The Overall PK Analysis Population was used for calculating PK parameters, including
decitabine daily AUC 0-24 , which was used to derive the primary PK endpoint of
185
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
decitabine cumulative 5-day AUC 0-24 exposures and other secondary PK parameters for
decitabine, cedazuridine, and cedazuridine-epimer.
Two additional analysis populations were used for sensitivity analyses of the PK
endpoints:
Evaluable unpaired PK population: the overall sensitivity population, defined as the
subjects who contributed PK data, excluding subjects whose samples had integrity
issues.
Paired sensitivity population: the subjects in the paired analysis, excluding subjects who
received only 1 cycle of treatment.
The following Analysis Sets were used for analysis of all non-PK secondary endpoints:
The Efficacy Analysis Set includes data from all subjects who received any amount of
study treatment. All data was included, and no subjects excluded because of protocol
deviations. Subjects were analyzed according to their randomly assigned treatment
sequence.
The Safety Analysis Set includes data from all subjects who received any amount of
study treatment. In the safety analysis, no data exclusion was allowed because of
protocol deviations. Subjects were analyzed according to the treatment sequence
received.
The PD LINE-1 Analysis Set includes data from subjects if they received any amount of
study treatment and had LINE-1 methylation data at baseline (Day 1) of Cycle 1 or 2 and
on either Day 8 or Day 15 of the respective cycle.
Methods for Handling Missing Data (Phase 3)
BLQ Data
All plasma concentrations that were below the lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) or
missing were labeled as below the limit of quantification (BLQ) or missing, respectively,
in the concentration data listings. Considering the number of PK samples to estimate
AUC 0-24 , BLQ values were set to 0 for statistical summary of concentrations and
calculation of PK parameters.
Unless otherwise noted, data summaries were performed on only observed data.

186
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Missing Incomplete Dosing Information
In cases in which subjects were dosed with any of the study drugs but discontinued
treatment early, the concentration data were reported in the concentration tables and
included in all summary statistics and PK analysis if sufficient concentration data were
available to permit PK analysis for that treatment in that subject.
Missing Sampling Dates and/or Times
For concentration-time profiles from subjects with missing sample dates and/or times,
the nominal sampling day and/or time (as appropriate) were assumed for the
calculation of PK parameters.
Unexpected Data and Potential Scenarios for Exclusion
Every effort was made to include as many of the subjects as possible in the Primary
Endpoint PK Analysis Population for the primary analysis. Exclusions of subjects from the
primary analysis set were expected to be rare and could have happened under the
following circumstances:
-

The subject did not complete two full cycles of treatment and PK assessments to
include both IV decitabine and ASTX727 cycles.

-

The PK profile for the subject manifests as an extreme outlier versus profiles of the
remaining population, with an atypical profile possibly a result of contamination
during collection of blood samples (i.e., collection from the second lumen of the
peripherally inserted central catheter, instead of contralateral arm, following IV
infusion).

-

Atypical and unexplained PK profile for a subject rendering extrapolation of AUC to
24 hours not possible due to inability to calculate lambda-z.

-

Atypical decitabine PK profile influenced by vomiting after ASTX727 dosing.

The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s description of the SAP of the Phase 3 Study (ASTX727-02).

Patient Disposition (Phase 3)
Data:
A total of 138 subjects were randomized in the Phase 3 study from 30 study centers in
the US and 7 in Canada. The Efficacy Analysis Set and the Safety Analysis Set were
composed of the 133 treated subjects. All randomized and treated subjects were
included in the efficacy and safety analyses datasets. Five subjects were randomized but
not treated (3 subjects withdrew prior to treatment, 1 withdrew due to elevation of
bilirubin and creatinine before treatment, and 1 subject was lost to follow up after
randomization).
As of the CSR data cutoff date (19 March 2019), 94 subjects were alive and ongoing
treatment. The median duration of follow-up was 155 days (~5 months; range 57 to 397
days). The median duration of treatment was 4 cycles (range, 1 to 11 cycles).

Protocol Violations/Deviations (Phase 3)
Data :
Important deviations as determined by the Astex medical monitor and PK expert were
those that affected a subject's rights, safety or welfare, and/or the integrity of the study
data. A tota l of 15 subjects had at least 1 important protocol deviation. Categories of
important deviations, numbers of subjects in each category, and brief descriptions are
as foll ows:
•

Eligibi lity criteria: 2 (1 subject had a 27-day washout from prior azacitidine treatment
instead of 28-day and 1 subject was hospita lized more than 2 days within 30 days of
screening)

•

Study drug administration: 1 (94 ml of IV decitabine infusion was given rather than
the full 108 ml)

•

Study procedures: 9 (various PK and LINE-1 sample coll ection time points missed)

•

Safety: 3 (SAEs not reported to Astex with 24 hours)

The Applicant's Position:
These protocol deviations did not affect the study outcome or conclusions.

The Applicant’s Position:
Baseline disease characteristics of subjects in this study are representative of the
patient population intended to receive the marketed product. All IPSS risk categories
were represented and patients with CMML were also included.
Baseline disease characteristics were generally balanced between the treatment
sequences, but there were some imbalances that may confer worse prognosis for
patients randomized to Sequence A vs Sequence B. Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1)
compared with Sequence B (IV decitabine in Cycle 1) had a notably lower representation
of CMML subjects (7.6% vs 16.4%), a higher representation of IPSS High-Risk MDS
subjects (21.2% vs 10.4%), more subjects with low baseline neutrophils (42.4% vs 31.3%
<1000/μL), and fewer subjects with high baseline neutrophils (37.9% vs 49.3%
>1500/μL).

Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use (Phase 3)
Data :
During the 2-way crossover, 129 subjects (97%) received both cycles of treatment (oral
and IV).
The median number of cycles received was 4 (range, 1 to 11) and 39 subjects (29.3%)
received 6 or more cycl es. Eighty-two subjects (61.7%) had dose delayed cycles, and
27 .1% had dose reduced cycles. However, the study is ongoing and the median follow
up at the time of data cutoff was on ly 5 months.

The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment compliance was well controlled/managed at the study centers during the
first 2 cycles as all treatments (ASTX727 and IV decitabine) were administered by study
center personnel.
Dose reduced cycles and treatment cycle delays were permitted for management of
cytopenias. Overall, most (72.9%) subjects did not have a dose reduced cycle during the
Phase 3 study as of the CSR data cutoff. For the primary PK endpoint, only subjects who
received both treatments (ASTX727 FDC tablets and IV decitabine) and had adequate PK
samples to be able to calculate 5-day AUC for both treatments were included in the
primary paired PK analysis. No formal statistical comparisons between treatments (oral
and IV) were performed for efficacy endpoints.

The prima ry study endpoint for this study was a PK endpoint (total cycle 5-day
decitabine AUC) . The results of the comparison of 5-day decitabine AUC (ora l vs IV),
incl uding sensitivity ana lyses, are presented above in Section 6.3 .1 (Table 6). In this
Phase 3 study, efficacy endpoints were secondary (Table 37) .

The primary objective of this study was achieved and demonstrated that the decitabine
exposures (5-day AUC) ach ieved from ASTX727 FDC tablets was equiva lent to IV
decitabine. The preliminary efficacy results in th is study are supportive of the efficacy
results ach ieved in the Phase 2 study and are presented below.

Data Quality and Integrity (Phase 3)
Data:
The Astex Quality Assurance group audited 5 centers from the 37 centers that
randomized subjects. No critical audit findings were identified at 4 of the centers
audited. Appropriate corrective and preventive actions were taken for all audit findings.
Data integrity concerns were identified at one center (Site 217) as part of a for-cause
audit. These findings were reported to the OSI on 21 November 2019.
The data in this submission are in eCTD format, in accordance with FDA guidance on
electronic submission. The data sets are well documented and include definition files.
The data sets, clinical study reports, summary of clinical safety, and summary of clinical
efficacy are located in Modules 5 and 2 of the eCTD. \\CDSESUB1\evsprod\NDAnnnnn.
The Applicant’s Position:
The PK data collected at Site 217 was not useable and the PK data collected from the
2 subjects randomized at this site were not included in the primary PK analysis.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
No issues were identified with the data quality or integrity from the pivotal study which
could affect the efficacy results, aside from the issues with the response assessments
noted above. The submitted datasets are generally consistent, and variables are clearly
labeled and/or explained.
Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints (Phase 3)
Data:
Efficacy was not a primary endpoint in Phase 3. Efficacy results presented in this section
are considered preliminary at this time, as almost half the subjects had not completed
6 months of follow-up as of the data cutoff date for the CSR (median duration of followup was ~5 months). As of the cutoff date for the primary CSR, only response rates and
transfusion independence were evaluated and reported in the Phase 3 CSR.
Long-term efficacy endpoints (duration of response and time to event endpoints) were
not assessed or reported in the CSR. Exploratory analysis of these events based on the
cutoff date for the CSR are reported in this document and will be updated when all
subjects complete a minimum of at least 12 months of follow-up.

Transfusion Independence (Phase 3)
Transfusion independence post-treatment was defined as no transfusions for ≥56
consecutive days while maintaining hemoglobin ≥8 g/dL and platelets ≥20×109/L.
Overall, of the 55 patients who were RBC or platelet transfusions dependent at baseline,
32.7% were transfusion independent post-baseline as of the cutoff date for the CSR.
One-third (32.7%) of the 52 subjects who were RBC transfusion dependent at baseline
were RBC transfusion independent post-baseline. A 30% rate of platelet transfusion
independence post-baseline was observed for the 10 subjects with platelet transfusion
dependence at baseline.
Transfusion independence data by each response category are summarized in Table 39.
Despite the limitation of the short follow-up to assess transfusion independence all
patients with CR (100%) were transfusion independent post-treatment.

The preliminary efficacy resu lts in Phase 3 were secondary endpoints in the study. Only
response rate and transfusions in the Phase 3 CSR were assessed at the time of the data

The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
At the time of the initial submission, the median duration of follow-up on the phase 3
Study ASTX727-02 was only 5.1 months, compared to 24.0 months for Study ASTX727­
01-B. The Applicant submitted updated efficacy data for Study ASTX727-02 per the
prespecified statistical analysis plan at the same time as the 120-day safety update. At
that time, the median duration of follow-up was 12.6 months and the median duration
on treatment was 8.1 months. FDA's analysis of the updated efficacy results on the
Phase 3 trial are presented in the Table below.

We were in agreement with the Applicant's assessment of the complete remission rate
in the updated efficacy data set. FDA adjudicated CR responses based on evaluation of
bone marrow and laboratory results in the submission.
Again, the acceptable response rate for MOS for regulatory decision making is ORR,
defined as CR+PR responses. There were no PR responses on the Phase 3 trial of
ASTX727, and therefore, only CR responses were considered for demonstration of
clinical benefit. Lesser responses than CR/PR do not convey clear clinical benefit to
patients. Overall, the CR responses seen on Phase 3 mirror that observed with IV
decitabine.
We again note that the Phase 3 trial used IWG 2006 MOS Response Criteria, which
differs from that used in the IV decitabine prescribing information (IWG 2000 MOS
Response Criteria) (Cheson, Bennett et al. 2000). However, even assuming a slightly
higher CR rate than the 9-15% reported in the IV decitabine USPI with the use of

Dose/Dose Response (Phase 3)
Data:
A fixed dose was used in this study to produce decitabine exposure equivalent to IV
decitabine at the approved dose of the 5-day regimen, and assessment of response by
dose was not evaluated. The recommended dose and regimen for ASTX727 is 100 mg
cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine given orally (as the ASTX727 FDC tablet) once dai ly
o n Days 1 through 5 of each 28-day cycl e. In addition, ASTX727 treatment at the
recommended dose and regimen achieved almost identical demethylation to IV

decitabine, as measured by % LINE-1 demethylation (Table 8).
The Applicant’s Position:
No studies were conducted to assess dose-response relationship.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s assessment.
Durability of Response (Phase 3)
Data:
The efficacy results presented for the Phase 3 study are preliminary and are based on
available data as of the cutoff date for the primary CSR. Overall survival was not
assessed in the first analysis for this study. Responses are ongoing in the study and with
the K-M method median duration of response was not estimable. At the data cutoff for
the first analysis in this study, all CR patients have not yet progressed and only one
patient with mCR has progressed, so the duration of response for patients in CR or mCR
are preliminary underestimates. With that caveat, the median (range) duration of
response was 113+ days (29+, 306+) for subjects with a best response of CR, and 63+
days (range 4+, 320+) for subjects with best response of mCR.
The Applicant’s Position:
Duration of response and overall survival for the Phase 3 study will be assessed in the
second analysis of efficacy after all subjects have completed a minimum of 12 months of
follow up. Almost all patients with CR or mCR have not yet progressed at the data cutoff
of the first analysis in Phase 3. Durability of response is presented above in Section 8.1.1
for the Phase 2 study.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA performed an assessment of duration of CR based on the updated efficacy data
from the 120 day update (see FDA assessment Table above for Study ASTX727-02 Phase
3). The median duration of response is now 7.5 (range 1.7-17.5) months. This is similar,
but slightly lower than that observed on the Phase 2 trial. It is likely that the median will
increase over time with longer follow-up. Of the 28 CR responses, there were only 4
relapses to date, with most responses ongoing at the data cutoff date.
Of note, the duration of CR+PR in the IV decitabine label was 288 days (range 116-388),
which is equivalent to 9.4 months (range 3.8-12.8) months on the controlled trial and
443 days (range 72-722) days or 14.6 months (range 2.4-23.7) months on the single-arm
trial.

Using the updated efficacy data, we determined the median time to CR was 132 days

Integrated Review of Effectiveness
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
See below sections for our integrated assessment of effectiveness.
Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
The Applicant’s Position:
The approach to establish the safety and effectiveness of ASTX727 FDC tablets (100 mg
cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine) was based primarily on a pharmacokinetic (PK)
bridging approach to IV decitabine. In this application, the primary efficacy results are
from the Phase 2 (ASTX727-01). Supportive efficacy results from the Phase 3 study
(ASTX727-02) are provided.
All long-term efficacy endpoints, such as survival, are summarized from the Phase 2
study results only (Section 8.1.2). Due to the shorter duration of follow-up in Phase 3,
only preliminary results for response rate and transfusion independence are presented
here.
Complete blood counts (CBC) were completed prior to every cycle of treatment in both
studies. Bone marrow was assessed every other cycle at least up to Cycle 7 until disease
progression or treatment discontinuation. Transfusion needs were assessed
continuously during the treatment period. In Phase 2, response was evaluated by
medical review and statistical programming conducted by Astex (see implementation
guide in the SAP). In Phase 3, response was assessed by an Independent Review
Committee (IRC) composed of 2 disease experts (and adjudicated by a third, when
necessary) who did not participate as investigators in the trial (see IRC charter). The IRC
also assessed the categories of Stable Disease and Progressive Disease in the Phase 3
study, while in Phase 2 these categories were presented collectively as No Response.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment. In addition, FDA notes that the follow-up
efficacy data from the Phase 3 trial received with the 120-day safety update added
assurance of the clinical benefit of ASTX727, based on comparable efficacy results to the
Phase 2 trial and that of IV decitabine.

Primary Endpoints
Data:
PK: Decitabine total cycle 5-day AUC was a primary endpoint in both the Phase 2 and
Phase 3 studies. The results of the primary PK endpoint for Phase 2 (5-day AUC 0-t ) and
Phase 3 (5-day AUC 0-24 ) are presented in Section 6.3.1 (Table 12 and Table 13 for Phase
2 and Table 6 and Table 7for Phase 3).
PD: LINE-1 methylation was a primary endpoint in Phase 2 and a secondary endpoint in
Phase 3. The LINE-1 methylation results are presented in Section 6.3.1 (Table 8 and
Table 9).
Clinical efficacy: Response rate was a primary endpoint in Phase 2 and secondary
endpoint in Phase 1 and 3. Response rates for all Phases are summarized below and
compared with published data from IV decitabine 5-day regimen (Steensma et al 2009),
which also used IWG 2006 criteria for response (Table 40).

Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, evidence of clinical activity with ASTX727 was observed in all phases of ASTX727
studies. Response rate was relatively low for Phase 1 due to a large percentage of
subjects who received prior HMA (46%). In Phase 2, the response rate was high with
21% reporting CR, and median survival of ~18 months. The data from Phase 3 are
preliminary, but it is worth noting the CR rate of 9% in all treated subjects and 12% in
evaluable subjects. There was also a high percentage of mCR (34.6%) that it is likely that
some of them will become CR with further follow up. This would be consistent with the
observation that median time to CR is usually relatively longer than time to mCR as
reported above for Phase 2 and 3. The primary PK endpoint of similar or equivalent
systemic decitabine exposures was consistently met in Phase 2 and Phase 3 within the
limitation of the smaller sample size in Phase 2. Also, Phase 2 and 3 produced consistent
data of similar effect on DNA methylation from ASTX727 and IV decitabine.
The overall response rate was 60% in Phase 2 and 49% to 64% in Phase 3 in all treated
and evaluable subjects, respectively.
The response rates observed for ASTX727 in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies are similar
to those reported for 5-day IV decitabine dosing (Steensma et al 2009; Dacogen USPI
2019), suggesting that the equivalent AUC exposures and similar effect on DNA
methylation observed for ASTX727 and IV decitabine produce the same meaningful
clinical activity.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA disagrees with the Applicant’s assessment of the efficacy results from the Phase 2
trial, as noted in Section 8.1.2 above. FDA’s adjudicated CR rate was 14/80 (18%; 95% CI
10-28%) with a median duration of response of 8.7 (range 1.1-18.2) months. The CR rate
on the Phase 3 trial using updated efficacy data from the 120-day safety update was
28/133 (21%; 15-29%) with a median duration of response of 7.5 (range 1.7-17.5)
months. Both are similar to the ORRs (CR+PR) reported in the USPI for IV decitabine,
which were 17% for the randomized trial and 16% for the single arm trial.
Again, the acceptable response rate for MDS appropriate for regulatory decision making
is overall response rate (ORR), defined as CR+PR responses. ORR served as the basis for
approval of IV decitabine. There were no PR responses on either the Phase 2 or Phase 3
trials of ASTX727, and therefore, only CR responses are considered for demonstration of
clinical benefit. Lesser responses than CR/PR do not convey clear clinical benefit to
patients.

Secondary and Other Endpoints
Data:
Transfusions: Data on transfusions were available from both Phase 2 and 3. In Phase 2,
50% of subjects who were RBC or platelet transfusion dependent at baseline were
transfusion independent for ≥56 consecutive days. In Phase 3, RBC and platelet
transfusion independence for ≥56 consecutive days was achieved in 32.7% and 30.0% of
subjects, respectively, despite the insufficient follow-up in the study at the time of this
analysis. Transfusion independence for both RBCs and platelets in those who were
dependent at baseline ranged from 33% in Phase 3 (short follow-up) to 49% in Phase 2.
The rate of transfusion independence with IV decitabine for RBCs was 33% in the
published data (Steensma et al 2009).

INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment regarding the transfusion benefit of
ASTX727. On the Phase 2 trial, of 41 patients who were transfusion dependent (TD) of
RBCs or platelets at baseline, 49% became transfusion independent (TI) of both RBCs
and platelets for at least 56 days post-baseline. Of 39 patients who were TI of RBCs and
platelets at baseline, 64% remained TI of both RBCs and platelets for at least 56 days
post-baseline. Using the updated efficacy data on Phase 3, the numbers were very
similar. Of 57 patients TD of RBCs or platelets at baseline, 53% became TI of both RBCs
and platelets for at least 56 days post-baseline and of 76 patients who were TI at
baseline, 63% remained TI for at least 56 days post-baseline. This data supports a
transfusion benefit of ASTX727 in patients with MDS and CMML.
Subpopulations
Data:
Due to the differences in duration of treatment and follow-up between the Phase 2 and
Phase 3 studies, efficacy data were not pooled for subgroup analyses. The evaluation of
the effects of major demographic factors (age, sex) and other relevant intrinsic and
extrinsic factors on the PK of decitabine or cedazuridine was performed and results are
presented above in Section 6.2.1. Assessment of response by different subgroups based
on age, sex, race, country, disease (MDS and CMML), and different MDS IPSS categories
were performed for Phase 2 and reported above in Table 25 to Table 26.
The Applicant’s Position:
Subgroup analyses were performed for efficacy endpoints (e.g., response rate) by
different subpopulations and clinical activity was observed in all subgroups within the
limitation of the sample size. In addition age, sex, body weight, renal function, and
hepatic function were assessed as potential covariates in a PopPK analysis and found no
clinically meaningful effects that would require dose adjustments (see Section 6.2.1 and
6.3.2). The effects of severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment on the PK of
decitabine and cedazuridine is unknown. Studies ASTX727-01 and ASTX727-02 did not
have sufficient racial diversity to inform potential correlations with regard to race or
ethnicity.
The assessment of response by disease type showed that ASTX727 was clinically active
in both MDS and CMML patients. Patients with CMML tended to have better response
(Table 27).
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
In general, interpretation of the subgroup analyses is limited by the small sample sizes.
214
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
The Sponsor noted a better response rate in CMML patients, which was observed in
Phase 2 (CR rate 5/17 [29%] CMML vs 9/63 [14%] MDS). However, the opposite was
observed using the updated efficacy data from Phase 3 (CR rate 2/16 [13%] CMML vs
26/117 [22%] MDS). This indicates that the efficacy of ASTX727 is likely similar in
patients with MDS and CMML.
Additional Efficacy Considerations
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
Regarding considerations on benefit in the post-market setting, while patients who
enroll on clinical trials tend to differ in various ways from the broader population of
patients with cancer, there is no indication that efficacy would be lower in patient
populations underrepresented on the Phase 2 or Phase 3 trials. Given that the therapy is
oral, it will be important for prescribers to monitor compliance and adverse events
closely.
Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
Data:
Substantial evidence of effectiveness of ASTX727 was established primarily through the
PK-bridging approach. Evidence of decitabine AUC equivalence between ASTX727 and IV
decitabine was established in the Phase 3 study (ASTX727-02). The primary evidence of
clinical benefit (CR) and durability of response was demonstrated in the long-term Phase
2 study with complementary clinical benefit based on preliminary efficacy results from
the Phase 1 and 3 studies.
Results from the clinical development program demonstrated that ASTX727 is
associated with the following benefits:
-

-

-

Oral ASTX727 resulted in decitabine 5-day AUC exposure that was equivalent to IV
decitabine with high certainty. The oral/IV 5-day decitabine AUC of the FDC in the
Phase 3 was approximately 99% with a narrow 90% CI (92.66%, 105.6%). The oral/IV
AUC comparison was robust, with all sensitivity and secondary oral/IV AUC
comparisons were consistent with the primary analysis, including all 90% CIs entirely
contained between 80% and 125%.
Data from the development program also showed a similar biological effect of
ASTX727 compared with IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles. Differences of DNA
demethylation as measured by LINE-1 assay was approximately 1% or less between
ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Phase 2 and 3.
Since efficacy from HMA treatment requires multiple (at least 4 to 6) treatment
cycles (Table 1), the long-term efficacy outcomes in the ASTX727 program were
mainly driven by ASTX727, as patients received only 1 cycle of IV decitabine. While
215
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
ASTX727 would be expected to result in similar efficacy to IV decitabine based on
the above equivalent decitabine systemic exposure and similar biological effect, it is
reassuring that the mature data from the Phase 2 study also support that
conclusion, as follows:
 CR rate of 21% with ASTX727 compared to 9% to 15% in the Dacogen USPI
2019 (3-day and 5-day regimen respectively) and 17% as reported in
Steensma et al 2009.
 RBC and platelet transfusion independence in about 50% of patients who
were transfusion-dependent at baseline compared to 33% reported in
Steensma et al 2009 for RBC transfusion independence.
 Median survival of 18 months compared to 19 months reported in Steensma
et al 2009.
The Applicant’s Position:
While decitabine plasma AUC exposures are largely considered to be the principal PK
determinant of both biological and clinical activity of decitabine, long interspersed
nucleotide element-1 (LINE-1) demethylation and safety data were collected in a
randomized design in the first 2 cycles of Phase 2 and 3 to confirm similar biological
activity and safety to further support the PK bridging approach to IV decitabine. In
addition, subjects continued to receive ASTX727 after the first 2 randomized cycles so
that long-term efficacy and safety data were also collected in Studies ASTX727-01 and
ASTX727-02. This provided additional support that clinical data from ASTX727 are
consistent with the expected clinical profile of IV decitabine from published data. LINE-1
demethylation was similar between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles. The
clinical safety was also generally comparable between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the
first 2 cycles. Long-term efficacy and safety results after multiple cycles of ASTX727 were
consistent with the efficacy and safety reported with decitabine IV using the 5-day
regimen (Steensma et al 2009). Results from studies with ASTX727 therefore support
the conclusion that 5-day decitabine AUC exposure is appropriate to use as a bridge to
clinical effects.
The availability of an oral HMA alternative will alleviate a significant burden to patients
and patient care givers by eliminating the need for monthly 5-day IV infusions in a clinic
or hospital setting that may need to continue for several months or even years in
responding patients. This may also avoid premature treatment discontinuation that
could occur as a result of the burden of multiple-day IV infusions every month,
particularly for the most elderly patients and/or for those who live far from a treatment
center. Premature HMA treatment discontinuation has been reported to result in early
relapse (Cabrero et al 2015). An oral treatment will also avoid potential safety issues
due to multiple subcutaneous injections or multiple IV infusions that may require the
establishment and maintenance of a central IV line, which could also result in additional
216
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
complications.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
The efficacy of ASTX727 has been established, based on the results of the Phase 2 and
Phase 3 trials in adult patients with MDS and CMML (for which IV decitabine is currently
approved). Patients who received ASTX727 (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine)
achieved a CR rate of 18-21%. Duration of CR responses were sufficiently durable and
roughly comparable (although slightly lower) than that reported for IV decitabine.
However, many of the responses were ongoing at the time of data cutoff, and thus, the
median duration of CR would be expected to increase with longer follow-up.
The CR benefit was supported by a transfusion benefit on both trials, which
demonstrated that 49-53% of patients dependent on transfusions at baseline achieved
at least 56 days transfusion independence post-baseline and 63-64% of patients
transfusion independent at baseline were able to maintain that transfusion
independence for at least 56 days post-baseline.

Review of Safety
Data:
The safety profile of ASTX727 in patients with MDS and CMML was primarily assessed
based on safety results from the Phase 2 portion of ASTX727-01 study (ASTX727-01-B)
and the Phase 3 study (ASTX727-02) where subjects were treated with the
recommended dose of ASTX727 (100 mg cedazuridine and 35 mg decitabine). The safety
data of all patients who received ASTX727 from the Phase 2 (78 patients) and Phase 3
(130 patients) studies were pooled (N=208). The key findings from the pooled analyses
are provided in the following sections.
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety profile of ASTX727 in patients with MDS and CMML was consistent with the
known safety profile for IV decitabine, and no new safety concerns were identified.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s approach of pooling safety data across Studies
ASTX727-01-B and ASTX727-02 given the similar study designs and patient populations.
Two patients from Phase 2 and three patients from Phase 3 were only treated with IV
decitabine during cycle 1 (i.e. never received ASTX727), and therefore, were excluded
from the safety set. This led to the total of 208 patients in the pooled population.

Overall Exposure
Data:
Overall, 208 subjects were exposed to ASTX727 in the Phase 2 (78 subjects) and Phase 3
(130 subjects) studies and 69 subjects were exposed to cedazuridine and/or decitabine in the
Phase 1 studies (Table 42 and Table 43). In the controlled clinical trials a total of 208 subjects
were exposed to at least 1 dose of 100 mg cedazuridine plus 35 mg decitabine administered
concomitantly as separate capsules or as the ASTX727 FDC tablet and a total of 159 subjects
were exposed to the ASTX727 FDC tablet (Table 44).
It is important to note that the first 2 cycles of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 controlled clinical trials
consisted of a 2-way crossover. Subjects received both ASTX727 and IV decitabine in these
trials. A total of 207 subjects received IV decitabine (Table 48).

Treatment intensity was calculate based on exposure data and number of days of treatment

intensity column. As per the Phase 2 and 3 study design, dose reduction was not allowed in the
first 2 cycles, however one subject in each of Cycle 1 and 2 did not receive the full 5 days of
treatment (one in an ASTX727 cycle and one in an IV decitabine cycle).

The Applicant's Position:
The number of patients exposed to the study drug in t he integrated analysis (208 patients) and
the total number of cycles (1342 cycles) with median of 5 cycles (range 1 to 29) is adequate for
safety review. The duration of follow-up in the Phase 2 (minimum: 12 months; median:
"'24 months) and Phase 3 (mi nimum: 2 months; median: "'S months) studies and t he extent of
exposure (i.e., number of cycles) are sufficient to assess the long-term safety of ASTX727. In the
Phase 2 study with t he lo nger foll ow-up, dose intensity was 80% or higher in over 90% of t he
patients in each cycle up to cycle 12. The data from Phase 3 show lower dose intensity beyond
5 cycles, but t he number of patients beyond 5 cycles are small due to the shorter follow-up.
This pooling strategy was chosen because it provides the largest dataset for safety assessment
and includes subj ects who received multiple cycles of t he same dose of cedazuridine and
decitabine, and because there were similar exposure profi les (based on decitabine AUC) and
adverse event (AE) profi les in ASTX727-01 Phase 2 and ASTX727-02 Phase 3. In addition, both
studies provided a safety comparison between ASTX727 and IV decitabine in the first 2 cycles of
treatment. Because Study ASTX727-01 Phase 2 was started earlier, t he duration of treatment
and follow up (median 7 cycles and 24 months, respectively) is longer t han in Study ASTX727-02
(median 4 cycles of treatment and 5 months follow up) (Table 42).
The Regu latory Authorities' Assessment :
FDA agrees with t he Applica nt' s assessment of t he pooled safety analysis set (SAS) of 208

subjects exposed to ASTX727 in the Phase 2 (n=78) and Phase 3 (n=130) studies, excluding the 5

the one cycle of IV decitabine was part of the larger treatment regimen on these studies, FDA
analyzed safety during all cycles of therapy, including the IV decitabine cycles. This differed
from the Applicant's analyses, where TEAEs with onset during the IV decitabine cycle were not
included in the "All Cycles" columns.
Furthermore, FDA focused its review of comparative safety between ASTX727 and IV decitabine
in the first cycle of therapy, so as to avoid the possibility of overlapping toxicities between the
first and second cycles of therapy. In these comparative analyses, we included the 5 subjects
treated with IV decitabine in cycle 1 only. A total of 106 subjects received IV decitabine during
Cycle 1 and a total of 107 subjects received ASTX727 during Cycle 1.

Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
Data:
The safety and efficacy popu lation in t he Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies were the same including

All MOS IPSS types were represented in the study, and 15.5% of subjects had CMML. IPSS low­
risk MOS was included in Study ASTX727-02 as it corresponded to eligibility by FAB disease
criteria from the Dacogen USPI indication. The median time since diagnosis was 66 days (range
5 days to 15 yea rs). Just over 20% of subjects had had prior anticancer therapy, with under 5%
having had 1 previous cycle of HMA therapy. Just over 40% of subjects were dependent on RBC
transfusions at baseline and just over 10% were dependent on platelet transfusions at baseline.
Low hemoglobin (<80 g/L) was present at baseline in 17.8% of subjects, 14.6% had low platelets
(<25x109 /L), and 22.5% had low neutrophils (<0.5x109 /L). Ha lf of the subjects had >5% blasts in
baseline bone marrow.
The Applicant's Position:
The demographic and disease characteristics of the integrated popu lation were representative
of the general disease popu lation for MOS of all IPSS scores and CMM L.

statement that under 5% had 1 previous cycle of HMA therapy. Reviewing the data in the
submission, we determined that 17 (8%) of patients in the pooled SAS received up to 1 prior
cycle of HMA therapy.

Categorization of Adverse Event
The Applicant’s Position:
The definitions for AEs and SAEs are provided in the individual study protocols. Disease
progression was not considered to be an AE or serious adverse event (SAE).
•
•

AEs that occurred during screening (prior to dosing) were recorded. Any new AEs from
the start of study treatment to the earliest of the following were recorded: Until 30
days after the last dose of study treatment or
Until the subject starts new MDS/CMML treatment (including new investigational
treatment)

AEs were collected spontaneously and were elicited at scheduled visits using an open-ended
non-leading question such as “Have you had any new symptoms, injuries, illnesses since your
last visit?”.
AEs and SAEs that occurred during the protocol-specified reporting period were followed until
1.) resolution, 2.) the investigator assesses the subject as stable and the event is following a
clinically expected outcome, or 3.) the subject is lost to follow-up or withdraws consent.
Verbatim descriptions of all AEs were mapped to MedDRA thesaurus terms for all studies in the
individual CSRs. For the integrated analyses, AEs from Phase 2 Study ASTX727-01 were re­
mapped using MedDRA version 22.0 (up-versioned from the versions used in the CSR) to be
231
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
consistent with the version used for coding AEs in Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02. Verbatim terms
as well as the preferred terms are provided in by-subject listings of Adverse Events.
Adverse event severity was graded using NCI Common Toxicity Criteria for Adverse Events
(CTCAE) version 4.0.3 for Phase 3 Study ASTX727-02. For Study ASTX727-01, CTCAE version 4.0
was used to grade AE severity. No re-grading of AE severity was performed.
All AEs summarized in the submission and summarized below are TEAEs. A treatment-emergent
adverse event (TEAE) was defined as any new AE or any worsening of an existing condition with
an onset date on or after the first study drug administration date (C1D1) and up to 30 days after
the last dose of study treatment or the start of an alternative anticancer treatment (for
MDS/CMML or subsequent leukemia), whichever occurred first. Events that occurred more
than 30 days after the last dose of study treatment or the start of an alternative anticancer
treatment (for MDS/CMML or subsequent leukemia) that were both serious and related to the
study treatment were also considered treatment emergent. AEs with unknown onset date were
considered to be treatment-emergent unless the stop date/time was known to be before first
administration of the study drug (C1D1).
All AE data collected in the clinical study database with an AE start date on or before the CSR
data cutoff dates were included in the data listings. However, only TEAEs are included in the AE
summary tables. The number and percentage of subjects who had AEs are summarized by
MedDRA system organ class (SOC) and preferred term (PT). AEs were not summarized based on
per patient-year).
Summary tables that provide the causality of AEs are based on the investigator’s assessment of
relationship to study drug.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment. FDA evaluated the mapping of verbatim AE terms
to MedDRA terms and is in agreement with the mapping.
We created a grouped terms dictionary based on common related terms (see Appendix 19.5).
The grouped terms are included in safety analyses throughout the review and will be indicated
with a “*” in safety tables and the text of the review.

minimum); hematology parameters were assessed more frequently in the first 2 cycles. The
schedule of assessments is provided in Table 18 for Phase 2 and in Table 31 for Phase 3.

Phase 1 Studies
As of the 01 June 2017 data cutoff date for the Phase 1 stage of Study ASTX727-01, 4 of 44
subjects died from AEs occurring up to 30 days from the last study treatment, all were
considered unrelated to treatment by the investigator. TEAEs with an outcome of death in this
study included:
• cardiac arrest (2 patients): died C7 D20; and C12 D10
• multiple organ dysfunction syndrome (died C1 D31), and sepsis (died C1 D20)
In Study ASTX727-04 (food effect), 1 of 17 treated subjects had an AE with an outcome of death
as of the 08 July 2019 CSR data cutoff. TEAEs with an outcome of death in this study included:
•

atrial fibrillation (died C1 D25) considered unrelated to study treatment

In the E7727-01 ADME study in healthy volunteers there were no deaths or SAEs during the
study.
The Applicant’s Position:
In general, AEs with an outcome of death were primarily infections that likely occurred as a
result of prolonged neutropenia, which are typical for this patient population. A few other
events, such as cardiac arrest, respiratory failure, and cancer were also observed, which are not
unusual given the age of the subjects in the study population. Fatal AEs reported in >1 subject
were sepsis and septic shock. In Cycles 1 and 2, there was no difference in the incidence of fatal
AEs between treatments. In the IV decitabine 5-day published study (Steensma et al 2009),
11 patients of 99 treated died within 30 days of study treatment (11%). The 6.7% deaths within
30 days of treatment in the pooled safety data set is consistent with data from IV decitabine.

As per the Table above, two cases that the Applicant designated as due to adverse reaction
(pneumonia) (n=l) and unknown (n=l) were deemed related to primary disease by FDA. These
case narratives are described in detail below.
61

Subject
(b)( : This was an 86-year-old man with CMML enrolled on the Phase 2 trial in Stage
2, Sequence A. On Study day 57, 24 days after the last dose of IV decitabine, the patient
presented to the hospital for bilateral pneumonia. The patient had pneumonia prior to starting
on the study, as well. He was found to have leukocytosis of 139.9 and was started on
antibiotics. He clinically deteriorated and was transferred to the intensive care unit for
respiratory failure and hyperleukocytosis and was intubated. Acute kidney injury, tumor lysis
syndrome, and severe coagulopathy were also noted. White blood cell count further increased
to 225.7. The patient died on Study day 57. The investigator assessed the event of bilateral
pneumonia as Grade 5 and not related. The Applicant concurred but noted that the cause of
death was the AE in the NOA dataset. FDA deemed the root cause of death to be the underlying
disease, as evidenced by the hyperleukocytosis and tumor lysis syndrome.
61

(b)( : This was a 66-year-old man with CMML enrolled on the Phase 2 trial in Stage 3
Subject
Sequence B. He was hospitalized on study day 264, 5 days after the last dose, with an unrelated
SAE of abdominal pain. On day 267, 12 days after the last dose, while still hospitalized, bone
marrow showed progression of CMML. He was transitioned to comfort care and remained in
the hospital for pain management. On Day 274, the patient died, with an immediate cause of
death reported as disease progression. Increasing peripheral blasts and pancytopenia may have
contributed. The Applicant listed the cause of death as unknown, but given the report of
suspected disease progression in the narrative along with increasing peripheral blasts, FDA
considered this death to be related to the primary disease.

Lastly, FDA reviewed the two deaths that occurred in patients treated with IV decitabine during
Cycle 1 (Subjects -n>nsi and -n>n61) who never received ASTX727. The first patient died of
an adverse reaction of septic shock on Day 28, 23 days after the last dose of IV decitabine and
the second patient died of an adverse reaction of pneumonia on Day 48, 43 days after the last
dose of IV decitabine.

INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Serious Adverse Events
Data:
The most common non-fatal SAEs in the integrated population across all cycles were febrile
neutropenia, pneumonia, and sepsis (Table 52).
In Cycles 1 and 2, the incidence of non-fatal SAEs was higher in the ASTX727 cycle (31.7% for
the overall pooled AST727 population) compared to IV decitabine cycle (21.3%). There was no
single AE responsible for this difference; however, small differences in the AEs of febrile
neutropenia (11.5% for overall ASTX727, 7.7% for IV decitabine), pneumonia (5.8% for overall
ASTX727, 3.9% for IV decitabine), and sepsis (3.4% for overall ASTX727, 1.0% for IV decitabine)
were observed. All 3 AE terms are associated with one another, since pneumonia and sepsis are
common in subjects with febrile neutropenia. In addition, the incidence of SAEs of pneumonia
and sepsis on ASTX727 during Cycles ≥3 decreased compared with the incidence on ASTX727 in
Cycle 1 or 2, to a comparable incidence with IV decitabine (3.8% for SAE of pneumonia in
ASTX727 Cycles ≥3 vs 3.9% for IV decitabine; 2.7% for SAE of sepsis in ASTX727 Cycles ≥3 vs 1%
for IV decitabine).

Due to the crossover design of the Phase 2 and 3 studies, there was a potential for AEs that
began and were reported in Cycle 2 to be a consequence of Cycle 1 treatment. This made the
Cycle 1 comparison more reliable as it is not affected by prior treatment. Therefore, an analysis
was performed to summarize SAEs by treatment for Cycle 1 and Cycle 2 separately (Table 53).
This allowed the assessment of AEs for Cycle 1, which was the principal randomized comparison
before the crossover, and the potential for carryover effects. The data suggest that the overall
higher incidence of SAEs reported in Cycles 1 or 2 for ASTX727 compared with IV decitabine was
mainly driven by a difference in the incidence of SAEs in Cycle 2 (35.6% for ASTX727, 19.8% for
IV decitabine) with a much smaller difference in SAE incidence in Cycle 1 (29.9% for ASTX727,
23.6% for IV decitabine). The small difference in SAE incidence in Cycle 1 may simply reflect
small differences in the population of subjects randomized to Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1)
compared with Sequence B (IV decitabine in Cycle 1). In Study ASTX727-02, subjects
randomized to ASTX727 in Cycle 1 (Sequence A) had a higher proportion with high risk MDS and
higher proportion with low baseline neutrophil count (<1000 μL) compared to Sequence B
(Table 35).

The Applicant’s Position:
Non-fatal SAEs from oral ASTX727 are mainly related to myelosuppression and infections
related to neutropenia, which is consistent with IV decitabine data from Steensma et al 2009
and Dacogen USPI 2019, and also consistent with expected complications of the disease itself.
The incidence was higher in ASTX727 Cycle 1 or 2 vs IV decitabine Cycles 1 or 2 in some of those
events such as febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, and sepsis (Table 52). However, the difference
was small and mostly in Cycle 2 for febrile neutropenia (Table 53). The differences were mainly
driven by Phase 3 population and may be related to lower baseline neutrophil count and higher
% of high-risk MDS in Sequence A (ASTX727 in Cycle 1) compared to Sequence B (IV decitabine
in Cycle 1) in the Phase 3 population. In Phase 2, the 2 most common SAEs of febrile
neutropenia and pneumonia occurred at similar or lower incidence in ASTX727 compared to IV
decitabine (Table 54). Only sepsis SAEs occurred at higher incidence in both Phase 2 and Phase
3.
The most common SAEs (febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, and sepsis) were identical in both
Phase 2 and Phase 3 and highly consistent with both the expected complications of the disease
population and the mechanism of action of decitabine. Myelosuppression and infections are
common complications in MDS and CMML and are also expected pharmacological effects from
decitabine. Since ASTX727 achieves equivalent systemic exposure and the same DNA
demethylation effect compared to IV decitabine, it is unlikely that the incidence of these events
are substantially different between ASTX727 and IV decitabine.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA performed an analysis of all treatment-emergent SAEs (TESAEs) and TESAEs in the first
cycle of therapy between ASTX727 and IV decitabine based on the data from the 120-day safety
update.
A TESAE occurring within 30 days after the last dose of ASTX727 or IV decitabine in the pooled
SAS (N=208) was reported for 142 (68%) of patients. Considering the first Cycle of therapy,
TESAEs occurred in 32 (30%) of patients treated with ASTX727 and 25 (24%) patients treated

Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
Data:
Overall, the incidence of AEs leading to discontinuation of treatment in the integrated
population was low (3.8%). During Cycles 1 and 2, two subjects each permanently discontinued
treatment with ASTX727 or IV decitabine due to an AE. Across all cycles, the only AE leading to
treatment discontinuation of ASTX727 that was reported in >1 subject was febrile neutropenia.
The Applicant’s Position:
Discontinuation of treatment due to AEs in ASTX727 is low and do not represent an impediment
to treatment compliance.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA performed an analysis of all TEAEs leading to withdrawal of study therapy in the pooled
SAS and by treatment arm during Cycle 1 using the data from the 120-day safety update.
TEAEs leading to withdrawal occurred in 11 (5%) of patients, including 3 (3%) of patients
treated with ASTX727 and 1 (1%) of patients treated with IV decitabine during Cycle 1. The only
TEAEs leading to discontinuation in more than one subject were febrile neutropenia (n=2) and
pneumonia (n=2). Overall, permanent discontinuation due to adverse reactions was quite low.
Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
Data:
The most common AEs that led to drug interruption or dose reduction of ASTX727 were
expected myelosuppression events, including neutropenia, febrile neutropenia, and
thrombocytopenia.
During IV decitabine treatment, there was one subject in Phase 2 who received 2 of the 5 days
of IV treatment during Cycle 2; dosing was interrupted due to an AE of sepsis.

Dose reduction

Significant Adverse Events

246
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NOA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
Myelosuppression
The most common Grade ≥3 AEs across all cycles in the overall ASTX727 integrated population
(N=208) were the expected myelosuppression-related events that could be related to either
disease or treatment with ASTX727, including thrombocytopenia (41.8%), neutropenia (42.3%),
anemia (28.4%), febrile neutropenia (23.6%), and leukopenia (20.7%) (Table 59). In Cycles 1 or 2
the incidence was generally similar between IV decitabine and ASTX727 (Table 59 and Table
60).
Infections
The most common Grade ≥3 infection AEs in the integrated population (N=208) was pneumonia
(10.1%) and sepsis (7.7%) for all ASTX727 cycles (Table 59). The incidence of pneumonia was
slightly higher for ASTX727 than IV decitabine in Cycle 1 but not in Cycle 2 (Table 60). Grade ≥3
sepsis was higher in ASTX727 Cycles 1 or 2, which is consistent with data from SAEs.
Phase 1
In Study ASTX727-01 Phase 1 (N=44), Grade ≥3 myelosuppression AEs included
thrombocytopenia (40.9%), neutropenia (29.5%), anemia (25.0%), febrile neutropenia (15.9%),
and leukopenia (15.9%). Most common Grade ≥3 infection AEs in Study ASTX727-01 Phase 1
included pneumonia (15.9%), and sepsis (6.8%).
In Study ASTX727-04 (N=17), Grade ≥3 myelosuppression AEs included neutropenia (58.8%),
thrombocytopenia (35.3%), anemia (29.4%), leukopenia (29.4%), and febrile neutropenia
(11.8%). There was 1 patient in this study with Grade ≥3 sepsis but no cases of Grade ≥3
pneumonia.
In Study E7727-01 (N=8), there were no myelosuppression AEs.
The Applicant’s Position:
The significant AEs of Grade ≥3 were the expected events of myelosuppression and infections.
They were consistent in all studies and as expected from the disease patient population and the
pharmacological effect of decitabine. The incidences were generally similar between IV
decitabine and ASTX727 in Cycles 1 or 2. A slightly higher incidence on ASTX727 mainly in cycle
1 was observed in some events, which may have also been related to the imbalance between
Sequence A and B in Phase 3. As discussed above patients randomized to Sequence A (ASTX727
vs IV decitabine in Cycle 1 in Phase 3) had worse baseline characteristics than Sequence B
(higher % of high risk MDS, lower % of CMML, and higher % of low baseline neutrophils
<1000/µL) (See Table 35 of patients characteristics in Phase 3). Many cases with sepsis occurred
in patients with Grade 3 or 4 neutropenia at baseline (Appendix 19.6, Table 86).

FDA performed an analysis of AESIs of cytopenias, infections, and hemorrhage in the pooled
SAS, including the comparison of ASTX727 and IV decitabine during Cycle 1 using the data from

To put thi ngs in perspective with these AESls, the USPI for IV decitabine usi ng the 15 mg/m 2
q8h x 3 days every 6 weeks dosing regimen reports an incidence of neutropenia of 90%,
thro mbocytopenia 89%, anemia 82%, febri le neutropenia 29%, and leukopenia 28%. Resu lts in
the USPI for the 20 mg/ m 2 qday x 5 days every 4 weeks dosing regimen were neutropenia 38%,
thro mbocytopenia 27%, anemia 31%, febri le neutropenia 20%, and leukopenia 6%. However, it
was noted that in this single-arm study, investigators reported AEs based on cl inical signs and
sympto ms rather than predefi ned laboratory abnormalities. Given t hat labo ratory AEs often
under-report laborato ry abno rma lities, it is difficult to make the comparison across tria ls.
However, the cytopenias seen with ASTX727 are rough ly t he same (in terms of febrile
neutropenia and leuko penia) or less (neutropenia, t hrombocytopenia, anemia) than what was
o bserved with t he 15 mg/ m 2 regimen of decitabine.
Fo r the 3-day and 5-day regimens, the IV decitabine USPI describes pneumonia in 22% and 20%
of patients and cellulitis in 12% and 9%, respectively. With t he 3-day regimen, 7% had
Staphylococcal infection and 5% had bacteremia and with the 5-day regimen, 8% had
Staphylococcal bacteremia. These infectious AEs are of similar incidence to w hat is reported
here with ASTX727. In terms of hemorrhage, the IV decitabi ne USPI notes petechiae in 39%,
ecchymosis in 22%, and hemato ma in 5% for t he 3-day regimen and epistaxis in 13%, petechiae
in 12%, contusion in 9%, and ecchymosis in 9% for the 5-day regimen . Therefore, t he numbers

248

Reference ID 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
for hemorrhage also appear similar to what is reported here for ASTX727.
Overall, the AESIs appear to be more common in patients treated with ASTX727 compared to IV
decitabine in the first cycle of therapy. It is possible, again, that this may have been due to
imbalances in baseline demographics. It will be important for clinicians to closely monitor
patients while on this oral form of decitabine given the possibility for significant cytopenias,
infection, and hemorrhage. Despite this being an oral therapy that will allow patients to spend
more time at home and away from the clinic, it will be important to have close follow-up for
laboratory testing and clinical monitoring.
Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
Data:
Common AEs (All Grades)
The most common AEs across all cycles in the overall ASTX727 integrated population were
consistent with the known AE profile of decitabine and included thrombocytopenia,
neutropenia, anemia, fatigue, nausea, constipation, leukopenia, febrile neutropenia, diarrhea,
and dyspnea (Table 57). The incidence of these events was similar during randomized
treatment with ASTX727 and IV decitabine in Cycles 1 and 2. There were also no clinically
notable differences in the proportion of subjects with AEs in the different System Organ Classes
(SOCs), with the exception of the Infections and Infestations SOC. However, the most common
AE in this SOC (pneumonia) occurred at a similar incidence for ASTX727 and IV decitabine.

Grade ≥3 AEs
In Cycles 1 and 2, the overall incidence of Grade ≥3 AEs was generally similar in the ASTX727
cycle and the IV decitabine cycle in Cycles 1 or 2 (Table 59). This was also generally true for all
SOCs with the exception of Infections SOC, but no large differences in the incidence of
individual infection events, with the exception of sepsis (1.0% IV decitabine, 3.8% ASTX727). Of
note is that GI AEs of Grade ≥3 were <2% and therefore not represented in the table. The most
common myelosuppression AEs Grade ≥3 tended to be higher on ASTX727 in Cycle 1
(thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, and anemia), but higher on IV decitabine in Cycle 2 (Table
60). However, febrile neutropenia Grade ≥3 occurred at a higher incidence on IV decitabine in
Cycle 1 and higher on ASTX727 in Cycle 2 (Table 60).

Common all grades AEs in supporting study ASTX727-04 (food effect) included neutropenia
(58.8%), anemia (41.2%), thrombocytopenia (35.3%), fatigue (29.4%), leukopenia (29.4%),
nausea (29.4%), and constipation (23.5%).
Grade ≥3 AEs in supporting studies ASTX727-01 Phase 1 and ASTX727-04 were generally similar
to those in the integrated population. Overall (N=44) the most common Grade ≥3 AEs in
ASTX727-01 Phase 1 included thrombocytopenia (40.9%), neutropenia (29.5%), and anemia
(25.0%). In ASTX727-04 (food effect), the most common Grade ≥3 AEs (N=17) were neutropenia
(58.8%), thrombocytopenia (35.3%), anemia (29.4%), and leukopenia (29.4%).
The Applicant’s Position:
The common all grade AEs and Grade ≥3 AEs were consistent with the expected AEs from IV
decitabine and are the common complications of the disease population. The overall incidence
of all grade AEs and Grade ≥3 AEs was generally similar between IV decitabine and ASTX727 in
Cycles 1 or 2 with few exceptions.
GI AEs either as a system organ class or individually were very similar between IV decitabine
and ASTX727 in the first 2 randomized cycles and the GI SOC incidence did not increase with
subsequent cycles (Cycles ≥3). GI AEs of Grade ≥3 were <2%.
In Phase 1, there was no obvious difference in AEs particularly mechanism related
myelosuppression between the lowest dose cohort 1 in Phase 1 (cedazuridine 40 mg/
decitabine 20 mg) and the highest dose cohort 4 in Phase 1 (cedazuridine 100 mg/ decitabine
40 mg) suggesting a broad therapeutic index across all doses tested.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA performed an analysis TEAEs based on the data from the 120-day safety update, focusing

FDA agrees with the Applica nt' s assessment that the common all-grade and Grade ~3 TEAEs are
consistent with the expected AEs from IV decitabi ne and are common complicat ions in the
disease population. TEAEs ~ 5% more common with ASTX727 in the first cycle of therapy
included thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, nausea, dyspnea, d iarrhea, d izziness, headache,
leukopenia, transaminase increased, contusion, and hypoalbuminemia. TEAEs ~ 5% more
common with IV decitabine in the first cycle included arthralgias, myalgias, mucositis, and
hypokalemia. Again, the increased cytopenias seen with ASTX727 during Cycle 1 may have been
d ue to some baseline imbalances between the two treatment sequences. The increased
nausea, diarrhea, and transaminase elevation with ASTX727 could be associated with the ora l
administration. However, almost all of the gastrointestinal toxicity was Grade 1-2 in severity.
FDA conducted an analysis of cases of acute febrile neutrophilic dermatosis (AFND, also known
as Sweet 's syndrome) given that this TEAE has been reported in the postmarketing setting for
Dacogen. There were t wo cases of Grade 2 AFN D noted in the subm ission - one in an 80-year­
61
o ld man with MOS on the Phase 2 trial on Cycle 2, Day 24 of ASTX727 (Subject
<bH ) and
o ne in a 66-year-old man w ith MOS o n the Phase 3 trial o n Cycle 2, Day 1 of IV decitabine
61
(Subject
<bH ). Therefore, FDA agrees with the Applica nt to incl ude this adverse reaction in
Sect ion 6 of label ing.
Lastly, FDA evaluated TEAEs on the healthy volunteer Study E7727-01. Of the 8 healthy
volu nteers enrolled and treated with two si ngle doses of cedazuridine 100 mg separated by a
washout, 5 (63%) experienced a TEAE. Two (25%) experienced nasopharyngitis, and one each
experienced anal hemorrhage, back pain, d iarrhea, and headache. All were of Grade 1 severit y,
except for back pain, w hich was Grade 2.

NDA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
In both Study ASTX727‐01 Phase 2 and Study ASTX727‐02 Phase 3 , hematology was evaluated
on Days 1 (Screening and/or Day 1 of Cycle 1), 8, 15 and 22 for the first 2 treatment cycles; and
at least on Day 1 of all following treatment cycles; and at study treatment discontinuation.
Baseline values of neutrophils, hemoglobin, and platelets were defined as the average of all
values obtained during screening up to and including the first dosing date. If there was only one
value available before treatment, it was used as the baseline value. In both studies, serum
chemistry was evaluated at Screening (for Cycle 1 treatment), then predose on Day 1 of Cycles
≥2, and at treatment discontinuation.
There were no clinically notable trends over time in central tendency for alanine
aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), serum creatinine, bilirubin, alkaline
phosphatase, or electrolytes (potassium, sodium, and magnesium).
Analysis of central tendency for selected hematology parameters (Figure 22 through Figure 24)
showed a decrease in mean neutrophils over the first 2 cycles followed by an increase, an
increase in mean platelets over the first 3 cycles followed by stable levels, and an increase in
mean hemoglobin over the first 8 cycles.

The incidence of Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) Grade≥3 laboratory
abnormalities for renal and hepatic function parameters was low, typically occurring in 1-3
subjects (<2%). Shifts of 2 or more CTCAE grades for renal and hepatic function parameters
were infrequent during treatment. Only 2 subjects had Grade 4 abnormalities (increased ALT
and AST), both of which occurred in the setting of fatal AEs.
Laboratory data were also evaluated for potential Hy’s Law cases. Three subjects met the Hy’s
Law laboratory criteria, but all 3 cases also occurred in the setting of fatal AEs of myocarditis or
sepsis. Narratives for each of these subjects were provided in the submission.
Common new or worsening laboratory abnormalities are summarized below in Table 62 (all
grades) and Table 63 (Grade ≥3).

The Applicant' s Position:
No cl inically meaningful trends in laboratory values were observed other than the trends in
hematology parameters as a result of the effects of continued treatment and also response to
treatment (increased platelets and hemoglobin). Laboratory abnorma lities of any grade
occurred in both IV and oral cycles and in all ASTX727 cycles with no clinica lly mea ningful trend
as incidence was somewhat higher on ASTX727 cycles for some laboratory abnormalities
(decrease albumin and sodium, increase glucose and alkaline phosphatase) but somewhat
higher on IV decitabine for others (bilirubin). Grade ~3 abnormalities ~5% were all
hematologica l with the exception of increase glucose in all ASTX727 cycles which was not
present in the first 2 cycl es for ASTX727. This is probably reflective of the elderly disease
population and underlying comorbidities. The hematological laboratory abnormalities of Grade
~3 were generally similar for IV and ASTX727 in Cycles 1 and 2.
The Regulatory Authorities' Assessment:
The Applicant's analyses of platelets, hemoglobin, and neutrophils over time indicates that
treatment with ASTX727 improved hematologic parameters with continued treatment. Of
course, this assessment is somewhat biased, as patients on continued therapy are more likely
to have responded to the therapy. However, the data is in-line with the demonstrated
transfusion benefit of ASTX727 in a subset of patients (see Sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.4).

and comparative safety population during Cycle 1 using the data from the 120-day safety
update.

*The denominator used to calculate the rate varied from 103 to 107 for ASTX727 Cycle 1, from 102 to 106 for IV
decitabine Cycle, and from 203 to 208 for All Cycles based on the number of patients with a baseline value and at
least one post-treatment value.

As expected, the predominate laboratory abnormalities were cytopenias. Again, the Grade 3-4
cytopenias were slightly more pronounced during Cycle 1 with ASTX727 compared to IV
decitabine, with the exception of platelet count decreased. The fact that the opposite was true
for the AE of thrombocytopenia (i.e. Grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia appeared worse for ASTX727
during cycle 1), and that the incidences of cytopenias were considerably lower using AE data,
illustrates that the AE data incompletely captures the incidence of cytopenias compared to the
more accurate and complete laboratory results. Therefore, cytopenias will be indicated in
labeling with the laboratory data.
A total of 3 patients (1%) in the pooled SAS experienced ALT and/or AST values > 3 x ULN
combined with a total bilirubin value ≥ 2 x ULN and alkaline phosphatase < 2 x ULN. FDA
reviewed narratives for all of the potential Hy’s law cases and ruled out drug-induced liver
injury based on plausible alternative causes, including sepsis (n=2) and myocarditis (n=1).
Vital Signs
Data:
In Study ASTX727-01, assessment of vital signs was performed at Screening, before dosing on
Day 1 of all treatment cycles, and at study treatment discontinuation. In Study ASTX727-02,
assessment of vital signs was performed at Screening, before dosing on all dosing days
(Days 1-5), on Days 8, 15 and 22 of the first 2 treatment cycles, on Day 1 of all following
treatment cycles, and at study treatment discontinuation. Vital signs were summarized for the
integrated population by visit using proportion of subjects with each vital sign being above or
below conventionally accepted vital sign normal ranges. In addition, for each vital sign
parameter, a figure of mean values by visit was provided.

262
Version date: June 11, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Reference ID: 4636063

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
NDA 212576, Original
INQOVI (decitabine and cedazuridine)
There were no clinically notable trends in the central tendency for vital signs parameters over
time, including heart rate, diastolic blood pressure, systolic blood pressure, respiration rate, or
temperature.
The most frequently occurring post-baseline vital signs finding outside the normal limits was
respiration rate, which was ≥20 breaths per minute on one or more occasions for
approximately 75% of subjects, and diastolic blood pressure, which was ≤55 mmHg on one or
more occasions for approximately 50% of subjects.
The most common vital signs AEs were dyspnea and pyrexia (20.7% and 11.5%, respectively,
across all cycles in the overall ASTX727 population), which were likely associated with the
occurrence of pneumonia and other infections.
The Applicant’s Position:
The vital signs findings are not unexpected in a relatively sick population with myelosuppression
who are receiving treatment for infections.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Sponsor’s assessment.

QT
Data:
A dedicated thorough QT study to evaluate the QT prolongation potential of ASTX727 has not
been conducted. Analyses of QTc from the pooled population and the Phase 2 and Phase 3
studies individual are presented in Table 65.
The pooled population (N=208) in Table 65 below includes QTcF interval data from both the
Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies. It is important to note that ECGs were collected at different time
points in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies. In the Phase 2 study ECGs were collected at
Screening and predose on Day 1 of each cycle (i.e., more than 3 weeks after last dose), and at
the Treatment Termination Visit. In the Phase 3 study, ECGs were collected at Screening and on
Day 3 (predose and 3 hours after ASTX727 and within 10 min before end of IV infusion for
decitabine) of Cycle 1 and 2. Due do the differences in ECG collection times between the
studies, an analysis of QTcF was also provided below for each study individually.
Maximum post-baseline absolute QT findings in Phase 2 and 3 presented in Table 65 probably
reflect the background noise of the disease population (elderly age, comorbidities, concomitant
medication) rather than a treatment effect as they do not indicate which cycle or day of cycle
they were recorded. Table 66 shows adverse events of QT prolonged in Phase 2. All patients,
except 2 had the QTc increase on Day >15 from start of cycle and almost all continued
treatment with no recurrence of events supporting that it is not related to drug exposure. The
two patients who had the event recorded on Day 1 of a cycle received multiple cycles (12 and
16) and the event has not been recorded in other cycles.
A summary of the QTcF analysis from the Phase 3 study is provided below in Table 67. This
tables shows the subjects with a new QTcF outlier value above 480 msec and above 500 msec
and the maximum post-baseline increase in QTcF done at the time of maximum drug exposure
on Day 3 of Cycles 1 and 2.

The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
As of the 120-day safety update, the incidence of all-grade PTs in the broad SMQ for Torsade de
pointes/QT prolongation was 16/208 (8%). This included 7 (3%) patients with electrocardiogram
QT prolonged, 7 (3%) with syncope, and one patient each with cardiac arrest, multiple organ
dysfunction syndrome, and sudden death. Of note, there were no Grade 3 or higher TEAEs of
electrocardiogram QT prolonged. No further ECG data were available from the 120-day safety
update.
Overall, the data presented are not adequate to determine whether there is an effect of
ASTX727 on post-baseline QTcF. Given the similar exposures to IV decitabine, the decitabine
component is unlikely to contribute to increases in QTcF, but there are no clinical data to
conclude that cedazuridine does not have an independent effect on QTcF. A PMR study will be
requested to evaluate the QT prolongation potential of cedazuridine.
Immunogenicity
Data:
No findings from overall safety AE assessments to suggest immunogenicity from ASTX727.
The Applicant’s Position:
No immunogenic reactions are expected with decitabine and were not observed with ASTX727.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment.
Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
Data:
No specific safety issues were observed in this submission that are not consistent with
decitabine reported data and its mechanism of action.
The Applicant’s Position:
No specific safety issues or new safety signals were identified with ASTX727.
The Regulatory Authorities’ Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment.
